    Unscheduled Work Register¶

    Status: Active | Version: 1.0 | Created: 2026-07-28

    Companion to the capability register (what the product must do) and the R1 deferred-debt register (what R1 knowingly deferred). This register holds the third category: work that was directed in some document but never scheduled anywhere.

    Why this register exists¶

    The 2026-07-28 sweep found a structural gap rather than an oversight. This project maintains four independent ID namespaces:

    Namespace Lives in Mapped into roadmap phases?
    K* / G* / A* / S* capability-register.md 🟡 Mostly, via roadmap.md's "Where every open register item lands" table. Written here as ✅ on 2026-07-28; the linkage check then proved 11 open capabilities (K1, K9, K10, K11, K20, G14, G18, A2, A3, A16, S8) appeared in no row of it. They are now mapped, as unratified proposals
    C* / GS* / U* / T* / P* / SL* r1-deferred-debt-register.md ❌ No: zero debt IDs are cited in either master document
    AL-* authoring-lessons-log.md ❌ No: zero AL- hits in either master document
    GitHub issues the tracker ❌ Partially: 19 of 33 open issues appear in no planning document

    Only the first namespace has phase linkage. This register closes that gap by assigning a phase to every unmapped item, and by giving items that had no ID at all (ADR follow-ons, design-doc open questions, code-level deferrals) their first stable identifier.

    How to use it¶

    • Adding work: if you defer something, add a UW-* row here in the same change, or cite an existing register ID. A deferral with no ID and no row is how items got lost before.
    • Doing work: move the row's Status to done and cite the PR. Do not delete rows; the audit trail is the point.
    • Dedup discipline: several docs describe the same findings under different ID schemes. Where a row says "canonical", that is the ID to use; the alternatives are cross-referenced, not duplicated.
    • Phase column: proposed home, not a commitment. blocked and decision are real states, not a scheduling failure; recording that something cannot be scheduled yet is the point.

    Status values: unscheduled (needs a phase commitment) | blocked (a named prerequisite is open) | decision (waits on an owner ruling) | verify (may already be done; confirm then close) | done (closed, with a PR reference).

    The linkage contract¶

    This section is the enforced contract, not advice. scripts/check_work_linkage.py reads it as the specification and fails the build on a violation. Changing the rules means changing that script and its tests, deliberately, in the same change.

    The invariant¶

    Every row in every register must resolve to exactly one disposition:

    Disposition Means Required evidence
    scheduled has a home in the plan Phase holds a value from the vocabulary below
    blocked cannot be scheduled yet the row names the open prerequisite
    decision waits on a human ruling the row names the owner who must rule
    verify may already be done the row names what to check
    done closed the row cites a PR, commit, or issue

    A row with a status but no evidence, or with a Phase value outside the vocabulary, is an orphan. Orphan count is the health metric for this whole system: it goes to zero and a check keeps it there. Everything else here is plumbing in service of that one number.

    This generalizes the rule scripts/check_lessons_log.py already enforces on the lessons log: a status asserts something happened, so it must cite what proves it.

    Phase vocabulary (closed set)¶

    Group Allowed values Source of truth
    Product phases 0 1 2 2b 3 4a 4b 4c 4d 5 roadmap.md ## Phase headings
    Track 2 phases 6 7 8 9 PROJECT-PLAN.md Track 2
    Milestones M0..M7, and dotted sub-milestones such as M4.1 roadmap.md Milestones
    Release rungs R1 R2 R3 roadmap.md release ladder
    Named workstreams content roadmap.md Content workstream
    Queue now roadmap.md Now queue

    Non-phase dispositions (closed set)¶

    Not every piece of directed work belongs to a product phase. These sentinels are legitimate, and being a closed set is what stops them becoming a junk drawer:

    Sentinel For
    CI hygiene repo tooling and gates; no product phase
    doc a documentation correction with no code change
    recurring an ongoing practice, not a one-time deliverable
    post-launch deliberately deferred past R3
    external:<repo> owned by another repository, for example external:homelab-infra
    issue:<number> a live defect tracked in the issue tracker rather than the phase plan, for example issue:460

    Not allowed¶

    • A cross-reference in the Phase column (see G, see H). Point at a real phase, or set the status to blocked and name the blocker. "See another cluster" is not a disposition.
    • An empty Phase on a row whose status is unscheduled.
    • The same ID in two namespaces.
    • More than one value in the Phase column. Work that spans phases takes the earliest phase, where it starts, and says so in the Item text. A comma-list hides which phase owns the commitment, which is the failure this register exists to prevent.
    • The Phase column repeating the Status value. decision and blocked are statuses; a row in either state still needs the phase it will land in once resolved.

    How the other three registers link in¶

    They keep their own schemas, because they track different things and flattening them would lose information. This register is the join table that gives their rows a phase:

    Register Linkage obligation
    capability-register.md a row not marked ✅ must appear in roadmap.md's "Where every open register item lands" mapping
    r1-deferred-debt-register.md a row not marked [Closed] or [Resolved] must be cited by a UW-B* row here. That register uses both markers interchangeably (3 and 7 rows respectively as of 2026-07-28); the validator honours both, because treating [Resolved] rows as open would report closed work as a gap and train readers to ignore the check
    authoring-lessons-log.md a lesson whose status is not applied, rejected, or superseded must be cited by a UW-C* row here

    That is why clusters B and C exist. They are not commentary; they are the linkage layer, and the validator treats a missing entry there as a build failure.

    Validate¶

    uv run python scripts/check_work_linkage.py

    Cluster A: ADR follow-ons¶

    Decisions that were accepted or proposed, whose consequent work was never scheduled. Sourced from docs/planning/adr/.

    ID Item ADR Phase Status
    UW-A01 Tiered RLS scoping in its entirety: Tier-1 scoped policies, set_config per request, the spike, and the integration suite run as cyo_api. Zero references in any planning document. 022 5 unscheduled
    UW-A02 Demote any Tier-1 table lacking a flat family_id; author the denormalization migration 022 5 unscheduled
    UW-A03 Production cutover (issue #559): retire postgres-role traffic. Closed 2026-08-04, verified live against production (cvrnaydpzijtszfbsraq): pg_authid shows cyo_api/cyo_worker both LOGIN, password set, rolbypassrls=f; pg_stat_activity shows 5 active app connections as cyo_api and zero as postgres, so the switch is observed in traffic, not just provisioned. Sequenced safely: PR #560 (eb96f687, sets Tier 1 RLS context before the device-grant lookup) was merged and re-verified green on e2e-staging.yml (multiple 2026-08-03 runs) before the homelab team rotated the production passwords, so the cutover did not reproduce the device-grant 401 that first surfaced on staging. The one leg left unconfirmed by that snapshot, cyo_worker showing 0 active connections because workers connect on demand rather than continuously, is now closed two independent ways, both 2026-08-04. First, a read-only probe run inside the live cyo-adventure-worker container through the application's own get_worker_session() returned current_user and session_user both cyo_worker, with rolbypassrls = false, and pg_stat_activity read from that same connection showed cyo_api on 5, cyo_worker on 1, and postgres on none; confirming the credential path directly made triggering a real generation job unnecessary, so no LLM quota was spent. Second, PR #608 replaced that one-off check with a standing one: the worker probes its own engine at startup and logs generation_worker.role_least_privileged or generation_worker.role_bypasses_rls, so from here on the leg is a log read after a worker restart rather than a pg_stat_activity snapshot timed to a live job. Read the fields, not the event name: WORKER_DATABASE_URL is unset on the deployed worker, so worker_database_url_effective falls back to database_url, and worker_dsn_explicitly_set: false is the field that identifies that state. One correction to what #608 assumed about that fallback, established by the live probe above and recorded here because the two landed within a day of each other: the fallback does not resolve to the API's role. DATABASE_URL is per container, and the homelab stack sets the worker container's own DATABASE_URL to the cyo_worker DSN, so the affirmative event is emitted as cyo_worker, reaching the right role by a different mechanism than the WORKER_DATABASE_URL split that docker-compose.prod.yml configures. Either way the outcome is least-privileged and the underlying finding is unchanged: the role is right by fallback rather than by design, which leaves get_session() and get_worker_session() indistinguishable inside the worker process. That is registered once, as UW-A44 landing with PR #609; do not open a second row for it here. Recorded in the R1 live e2e checklist sign-off for 2026-08-04. The ADR names M4.1 as its review gate; M4.1 carries no ADR-021 content. 021 M4.1 done
    UW-A04 worker_database_url + get_worker_session() + the three worker entrypoints. Delivered in c393baba (PR #334): core/config.py worker_database_url, core/database.py::get_worker_session, pinned against regression by tests/unit/test_worker.py:1045. 021 5 done

    UW-A41 Doc hygiene: PROJECT-PLAN.md section 3's ADR table and the ADR status list omit ADR-020 through ADR-024. Corrected by the 2026-07-28 audit in commit bcfc9ab; keep them in sync going forward. all now done
    UW-A42 Doc hygiene: backfill a Follow-on work section into all 24 ADRs that predate the adr/README.md rule requiring one, so the register can be reconciled against ADR-declared consequences rather than only against this cluster's own manual sourcing. all post-launch unscheduled
    UW-A43 Bind a child session to its minting device grant. Revoking a device grant stops the DEVICE token but not a child session already minted from it: api/deps.py::_child_principal does no database round-trip and the token carries no grant reference, so a child keeps reading online for the rest of child_session_ttl_seconds (12 hours). Fix: carry the grant jti in the child-session claims and check it on the read path, accepting a database read there. Disclosed in ADR-014 "Negative / risks"; current behavior pinned by tests/integration/test_child_sessions.py::test_known_limitation_revoked_device_grant_does_not_invalidate_minted_child_session. 014 5 unscheduled
    UW-A44 ADR-021's role split is enforced by container environment, not by the code path written for it, so a get_session() regression in worker code would be invisible in production. Found 2026-08-04 while confirming the last open leg of UW-A03. The worker reaches the right role: current_user inside cyo-adventure-worker is cyo_worker. But it gets there by fallback, not by design. WORKER_DATABASE_URL is unset on the deployed container, so worker_database_url_effective falls back to database_url, and the homelab stack (services/cyo-adventure/ in homelab-infra) happens to set that container's own DATABASE_URL to the cyo_worker DSN. This repository's docker-compose.prod.yml instead sets WORKER_DATABASE_URL explicitly, as commit 629a0263 intended, so the deployed stack and the committed compose file reach the same outcome by different mechanisms. Two consequences. First, inside the worker process both engines resolve to the same identity, so the get_worker_session() discipline that core/database.py documents as load-bearing is currently a no-op in production, and worker code that mistakenly called get_session() would still connect as cyo_worker and pass every observable check. Second, whichever mechanism is chosen should be the one that is tested, because right now neither file is authoritative. PR #608 lands a startup role probe that logs generation_worker.role_least_privileged or generation_worker.role_bypasses_rls, which is a real improvement to observability but does not close this row, and the reason is the whole point of it: that probe reports the role the worker connected as, and the role is cyo_worker whether it arrived by the intended split or by the container-env fallback, so it cannot distinguish the two and cannot see a get_session() regression in worker code at all. Its worker_dsn_explicitly_set: false field is the part that reads on this finding. To do: decide whether the container env or WORKER_DATABASE_URL is the contract, make the other match it, and add a test that fails when a worker entry point uses the API session factory (a static check over generation/worker*.py and covers/worker.py is enough, and unlike any live or startup probe it keeps working when the two roles coincide, which today they do). 021 5 unscheduled
    UW-A45 Nothing ties the emitted schema_version stamp to SCHEMA_VERSION, so ADR-025 decision 2's stamping clause and decision 3's converse are unenforced. Found 2026-08-06 during the final whole-branch review of the ADR-025 implementation (branch feat/persistent-characters). Storybook.schema_version defaults to SCHEMA_VERSION only when the field is absent from the input; every real producer supplies it explicitly and hardcodes "2.0" (the 61 committed skeleton documents under skeletons/; note skeletons/*/*.json matches 124 files, the other 63 being .contract.json/.lineage.json sidecars on a different versioning scheme, scripts/build_series_book.py, scripts/seed_dev_data.py, scripts/seed_series_catalog.py). Outside _normalize_legacy_fill, no Python in src/ ever assigns blob["schema_version"], but that scan misses the primary production path: the Stage-A prompt (generation/templates/structure.md:27) requires the model to emit schema_version and pins no value, so an LLM-generated skeleton carries whatever the model chose. That producer, not the hardcoded scripts, is the one this row's fix most needs to cover. (scripts/build_series_book.py:315 is also not a hardcode: it reads spec.get("schema_version", "2.0").) So decision 2's "It stamps newly-published blobs with the current version" and decision 3's converse (a story only carries a field defined at the minor it was published at) both hold today only because no minor beyond 0 has ever existed, not because anything enforces them. Recorded rather than fixed in this branch, since rewriting every producer was out of scope for a schema-versioning fix. Owner ruling given 2026-08-06: neither a publish-path stamper nor a hand-update sweep. Enforce the converse instead, with a validator rule requiring a document's declared minor to cover the fields it actually uses: if a document uses any field introduced at minor N, its schema_version must be >= N. This is deterministic, needs no producer changes, and leaves a plain 2.0 skeleton correctly stamped 2.0 rather than force-bumping every catalog file on each minor. It enforces decision 3 (a story only carries fields defined at its published minor) directly and makes decision 2's stamping clause unnecessary for correctness: an under-declared document fails the gate instead of being silently admitted. Scheduled into the ADR-025 follow-on plan alongside the SCHEMA_MINOR bump to 1; this row closes when that rule ships with tests. Implemented (2026-08-06) on feat/persistent-characters (PR #636), which delivers the declared-minor-covers-used-fields validator rule per the owner ruling above. Flips to done when that rule (L1-8) ships with tests. Shipped on this branch 2026-08-06: L1-8 (src/cyo_adventure/validator/layer1.py::_check_field_minors, registry src/cyo_adventure/storybook/field_minors.py) with tests in tests/unit/test_field_minor_floor.py, catalogued in docs/planning/validator-rules.md. Closed 2026-08-07: PR #636 squash-merged to main as 4fc65e5b, so L1-8 and its tests are on main and the citation survives the squash. 025 5 done
    UW-A46 Pathfinder Phase 0 pilot: one participating skeleton in a 13-16 gamebook cell, built to ADR-028's accepts_character envelope and proven by the CH-* validator rules. Owner ruled GO on SQ-22 / gate OG5 (2026-08-05); see story-structure-improvement-plan.md SQ-22/OG5 and pathfinder-structure-exploration.md section 8. Unblocked 2026-08-07: the CH-* rules landed on main in 4fc65e5b (PR #636), so the only remaining work is authoring the pilot skeleton itself. Scope note (2026-08-08): this row covers the pilot skeleton and its A/B measurement only. It does not cover the persistent-characters runtime (binding, replay, progression writeback, CRUD/API, UI), which is a separate body of work on branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime; an earlier revision of ADR-028 wrongly said this row was closed by that branch. First attempt withdrawn 2026-08-08: a 13-16 stat-envelope pilot was drafted and pulled from the branch rather than shipped, because with _check_dead_branches envelope-blind every stat gate had to use AL-129's XOR workaround, which leaves the reader unable to perceive the stat deciding anything and made all 14 stations mechanically identical. It is preserved out of tree. Second withdrawal, same day: the 8-11 archetype pilot (the-storm-chasers-club, promoted to Tier 2 on feat/persistent-characters-runtime) was withdrawn on 2026-08-08 for an unrelated mechanism, a tautological archetype gate that passed every CH-* rule (AL-131), so the shipped pilot turned out to be as imperceptible as the withdrawn one and neither pilot demonstrated a reader-visible envelope. Two pilots built to the same spec failed observability by two independent routes, which is evidence about the spec and the gate rather than about either book. Preservation risk, stated plainly: both withdrawn skeletons exist ONLY in gitignored locations today (.superpowers/sdd/held/, which .gitignore:323 excludes, plus a session scratchpad under /tmp). Neither is in git history on any branch: the withdrawal commit e16b4db4 removed the 8-11 promotion and the 13-16 pilot was never committed at all, so git log/git show cannot recover either one. A git clean -fdx, a scratchpad expiry, or a machine change destroys both with no warning and no recovery path. A tracked home would be the two skeleton JSON documents committed under a non-catalog path that the gate and the catalog loader do not scan (the catalog reads skeletons/<band>/*.json, so a sibling directory such as skeletons/withdrawn/ or an equivalent under docs/), each paired with a short note recording why it was withdrawn and which lesson (AL-129 for the 13-16 stat pilot, AL-131 for the 8-11 archetype pilot) explains the mechanism. That is a scope decision for the owner and is deliberately not taken here: re-adding the files reverses a withdrawal that was itself a reviewed decision, so it needs an owner ruling rather than a docs fix. Until that ruling, treat both artifacts as unrecoverable. This row therefore stays open, and whether authoring a pilot is worthwhile at all now depends on the envelope-awareness decision carried by UW-C64. 028 5 unscheduled
    UW-A47 A character-enabled book holds a synchronous run_gate call for tens of seconds against a fixed-size 40-thread pool, and nothing bounds how many can be in flight at once. Found 2026-08-06 while re-measuring the two #CRITICAL: timing markers on feat/persistent-characters (PR #636). Measured on the longwinter-station catalog skeleton (248 nodes, 51,241 base configurations): run_gate takes 0.77s with no envelope and 49.58s with the canonical 27-state stat envelope, a ~64x multiplier, because CH-3b and CH-4 re-walk Layer 2 once per entry state. Both api/node_edit.py and api/generation.py offload via await run_sync(run_gate, ...) onto AnyIO's default worker-thread pool (40 threads), and both routes are guardian-reachable. A book this size passing the CH rules is admissible, not an error case, so CH-5 and CH-8 do not catch it: they reject envelopes that are too large or graphs that are too expensive to expand, and this one is neither. Forty concurrent gate calls on comparable books exhaust the pool for roughly a minute, at which point every other run_sync caller in the process (not just these two routes) queues behind them. Recorded rather than fixed here because the fix is an architectural choice, not a tuning constant. To do: pick one of (a) move run_gate for character-enabled books onto the existing RQ queue so it never occupies a request thread, (b) give the gate its own bounded CapacityLimiter so saturation degrades these two routes instead of the whole process, or © memoise the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk, whose cost is dominated by re-deriving the same configuration set 27 times. Whichever is chosen, add a test that fails when a guardian-reachable route can hold a worker thread past a stated budget. Closed (Task 10 of the persistent-reader-characters workstream, ADR-028, branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime): took option (b). A single, process-wide anyio.CapacityLimiter (api/gate_limits.py::gate_limiter(), sized from the new gate_max_concurrency setting, default 4) now bounds both call sites via limiter=gate_limiter(); the two #CRITICAL: timing markers keep the 49.58s per-call measurement and additionally state the hold is bounded and by how much. This is concurrency, not latency: the 49.58s worst case is unchanged, so a queued request still holds its HTTP connection and may time out. Proof corrected 2026-08-08. As first written, this row cited tests/unit/test_gate_capacity_limiter.py as proof, but a whole-branch review found that two of those three tests passed unchanged with the limiter removed from both call sites, so the row asserted a proof it did not have; a test that cannot fail is not evidence, and a register row citing one launders it into the record. All three were rewritten to discriminate, and each was break-tested by deleting the property it names and confirming the failure. test_both_gate_call_sites_share_one_limiter now parses the AST of api/node_edit.py and api/generation.py and asserts each passes limiter=gate_limiter() to its own run_sync(run_gate, ...) call, rather than only that the singleton accessor memoises, which was true with or without a caller. test_calls_beyond_the_limit_queue_rather_than_spawn uses a threading.Barrier sized to total_tokens + 1: a correctly bounded limiter can never supply the extra party, so the barrier times out while the limiter holds and trips as soon as it is removed. test_the_gate_limiter_is_smaller_than_the_anyio_default_pool compares against AnyIO's live default pool size instead of a hardcoded 40, and asserts the le=39 bound on gate_max_concurrency (core/config.py:988) is enforced by pydantic rather than merely documented. Options (a) and © were not taken; ©, memoising the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk, is the only one of the three that attacks the 64x multiplier itself and is split out as UW-A48. 028 5 done
    UW-A48 Memoise the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk that drives run_gate's ~64x character-envelope multiplier. Split from UW-A47, which bounded concurrency (a shared CapacityLimiter, api/gate_limits.py) but deliberately left per-call latency unchanged: the 49.58s worst case on skeletons/16+/the-longwinter-station.json (248 nodes, 51,241 base configurations) with the canonical 27-state accepts_character envelope, against 0.77s with none, still stands. CH-3b and CH-4 re-walk Layer 2 once per entry state, re-deriving the same configuration set 27 times; caching that walk (or its derived configuration set) across entry states is the fix option UW-A47 recorded as © and did not take. Whichever queued request UW-A47's limiter defers still pays this cost once it runs, so this row is the one that actually shortens the hold. 028 5 unscheduled
    UW-A49 ADR-028's accepted residual: the bound seed can disagree with the seed the client opened from, and nothing reports it. Recorded as an accepted residual under ADR-028's "Integrity posture" section but given no register row, issue, or phase until now, which docs/planning/adr/README.md forbids for a consequent of an Accepted ADR. The mechanism: the active character can change between the client's seed fetch and _bind_active_character's call on the create path (src/cyo_adventure/api/reading.py), so the reading-state row is created carrying character B's seed_var_state while the var_state the client sends was produced from character A's. Nothing rejects it, because rejection needs a replay proof, replay needs a choice_path, and the shipped client sends none; a first save omitting choice_path is checked only against the structural floor. Two consequences, both recorded in the #EDGE marker on _bind_active_character in api/reading.py, which is the authority. First, user-visible today: on the read's next resume canGoBack fails closed against the mismatched seed, so Go back silently disappears for the rest of that read and RESTART reopens from the new character's numbers. Second, latent and worse: anything that starts sending choice_path (a native client, the offline queue, replay-based conflict resolution) converts this from a degraded Go-back button into a permanently wedged read, because every later save then replays from the stored seed, disagrees, and 422s. A related residual covers a fresh read that cannot reach the network, or is served a service-worker-cached characters response with no seed_var_state: it opens from declared initials. To do: decide the reporting posture before anything sends choice_path, since the escalation is triggered by that change rather than by time. The candidate fixes are (a) re-read the active character at bind time and reject or re-seed on a mismatch, (b) return the bound seed to the client on the create response so a divergence is detectable client-side, or © accept the degradation permanently and make it visible rather than silent. Blocks nothing on this branch; must be closed before UW-C19 (making the client send choice_path) lands, and UW-C72 is the other row that escalates on the same trigger. 028 5 unscheduled
    UW-A50 ADR-018 D6: a complete data inventory and processing map. Four documents each hold part of it (privacy-model.md, records-of-processing-activities.md, data-retention-policy.md, processor-dpa-checklist.md) and nothing reconciles them with each other or with the schema. The two missing columns are the load-bearing ones: provenance (collected from the child vs from the guardian about the child) and purpose (the specific product function that needs the element). Four obligations take the map as input and none can be discharged without it: 312.4(d) published notice, 312.4(b)/© direct notice, 312.10 retention, and the Rule's necessity limit. D6 decides the mechanism (generated from the ORM models; hand-maintained is not an acceptable form) and leaves the host document to the owner, with records-of-processing-activities.md the cheapest candidate since it is already per-activity and already lists recipients. Owner-side; not a counsel question. 018 7 unscheduled
    UW-A51 ADR-018 D7: the Information Security Program tracked as a continuing obligation rather than a completed artifact. 16 CFR 312.8 imposes a substantive, continuing duty; the 2025 amendment's addition is that it be written. information-security-program.md names an annual risk-assessment cadence, a vulnerability-testing cadence, vendor due diligence, and an owner, and nothing in this repository records that any cadence has actually run. Live instance: the 2026-07-28 ZDR change added AWS Bedrock, Microsoft Azure, and Google Vertex as OpenRouter sub-processors. Both registers were updated the same day, so the documentation leg fired correctly; what did not happen is the assessment. Each of the three processor-dpa-checklist.md rows still carries its own open item on its face (confirm OpenRouter's terms name and bind its sub-processors, and that ZDR routing holds at that endpoint). Also carries the internal-vs-published ruling: D7 holds the program is not a public artifact, which the counsel brief's Section 3 now reflects. 018 7 unscheduled
    UW-A52 ADR-018 D8: confirm the current scope of the internal-operations exception at 16 CFR 312.5©(7) before any consent-free free-tier engineering starts. The ADR entry is sourced from a pre-amendment (June 2023) document, so the exception's post-2025 scope is unverified, and the specific unknown that decides the design is whether reading-progress sync survives inside it. This is a decision gate, not an implementation row: the tiering work it would authorise is substantial (eight tables key to child_profile_id, and api/reading.py::_require_assignment gates reads on a server-side assignment row), and none of it should begin before the scope question is answered. 018 7 decision
    UW-A53 ADR-018 D9 and the wider US state-law gap. D3 confirms a US-only launch, which removes GDPR-K and the UK AADC but removes no US state law, and this ADR previously directed that residual nowhere. D9 makes it concrete for California SB 976, whose core provisions take effect 2027-01-01 and whose threshold question (are we a covered service at all?) is open. California's AB 2273 and every other state statute remain entirely unscoped. Owner to rule on scoping breadth before Track 2 opens intake beyond the family tier. 018 7 decision
    UW-A54 ADR-005 overstates the control it implements: correct the approval language. The ADR claims mandatory approval delivers "a hard guarantee that a parent saw every story" and estimates review at "a few minutes". The implemented surface presents flagged passages, then every reachable passage in order, then unreachable ones, and approval is a single state transition with no attestation, no per-passage acknowledgement and no reading requirement. At the oldest band the median whole-graph artifact is roughly 42 times the words on the path a child reads (median 42,700, maximum 118,475, against about 2,800), so "a few minutes" is achievable only as flagged passages plus sampling, which the ADR does not say. Supportable claim: publication requires guardian approval after the review interface has presented the book and its review evidence. Consequences beyond wording, and the reason this is not merely editorial: deterministic graph and state validation must carry more of the safety burden, prose obligation checks must produce a strong review worklist, and high-risk uncertainty should block publication rather than rely on an improbable full read-through. Raised by the third external review. 005 4b decision
    UW-A55 ExternalServiceError has no status of its own, so a KWS outage and a permanent misconfiguration are the same 400 to the browser. app.py::_STATUS_BY_EXCEPTION lists six exception types and ExternalServiceError is not among them, so it falls through to the 400 fallback alongside ConfigurationError and BusinessLogicError. On POST /api/v1/consent/kws/start that collapses two opposite situations: three permanent refusals (KWS unconfigured on this tier, no User row, no email on file) where retrying cannot help, and a vendor outage or timeout where retrying is exactly the right advice. GuardianVerificationPage.tsx::messageForStartError must pick one message for the merged code and picks "Trying again will not help, so please contact support", which is wrong advice for the outage case; PR #681 sharpens the cost by resolving a failed send to send_failed, so the retry the parent is told not to attempt would now be accepted immediately. Deliberately not fixed inline: giving ExternalServiceError a 502 is a repo-wide contract change touching every router that raises it, and the alternative (a machine-readable rule code in the error body) is a response-shape change with its own client-generation consequences. The failure direction chosen meanwhile is the safe one for a COPPA gate, since the opposite default loops a parent forever against a condition only an operator can clear. Marked with a #ASSUME/#VERIFY pair at the mapping site. Closed by PR #699 (branch fix/uw-a55-external-service-502): app.py::_STATUS_BY_EXCEPTION now carries (ExternalServiceError, 502); APIError, DatabaseError, and ProviderError (its only subclasses, none raised at a route boundary except ExternalServiceError itself today) inherit 502 through that one row via isinstance rather than needing rows of their own. The repo-wide audit the deferral called for found exactly two direct raise ExternalServiceError sites reachable from a route: api/covers.py::request_cover (an unreachable RQ broker) and consent/kws_client.py (every KWS send/auth failure, surfaced through consent/service.py to api/consent.py::start_kws_verification); 502 is correct at both, since both are genuinely retryable vendor/infra failures with no case where the old 400 was load-bearing. Both routes' error_responses() declarations were widened to document 502 (api/covers.py gained it alongside its existing 400; api/consent.py gained it alongside 400/403/409/429), and api/schemas.py::_ERROR_DESCRIPTIONS gained a 502 entry, which changes app.openapi()'s output for both operations. The committed frontend client (frontend/src/client/) was regenerated in the same commit against a schema dumped in-process the way ci.yml's contract job dumps it, so no live backend was needed and the drift job has nothing to find. The frontend RAD marker on CANNOT_SEND in GuardianVerificationPage.tsx is resolved and rewritten to describe the closed state rather than the open assumption; messageForStartError needed no new branch, since classifyApiError already treats any status >= 500 (502 included) as its server kind and the existing override already returns the retry-appropriate START_ERROR copy, so a 502 was correct by construction once the backend stopped returning 400 for it. Tests updated: tests/unit/test_app.py (ExternalServiceError/DatabaseError/APIError/ProviderError all now assert 502), tests/integration/test_consent_api.py (two tests asserting the old 400 now assert 502), tests/integration/test_cover_api.py (tightened a >= 400 assertion to == 502), and frontend/src/auth/GuardianVerificationPage.test.tsx (new case pinning 502 to the retry-appropriate copy, distinct from the 400/403 permanent-refusal case). Two consequences of the new status found in review and fixed in the same PR: (a) the 502 body was disclosing details.service_name and details.status_code, naming the upstream vendor and its HTTP status to the caller, so both keys joined app.py::_SENSITIVE_DETAIL_KEYS and are now server-log-only like value and context; (b) consent/kws_client.py was collapsing an operator-fixable credential rejection (401/403 from either KWS leg: a bad client id or API key, a lost grant, or the missing User-Agent KWS answers with a 403 "Request blocked") into the same retryable 502 as a transient outage, reproducing the "please try again" loop this row set out to end, so those statuses now raise ConfigurationError and land on the existing operator-problem 400 alongside "KWS is not configured". That also narrows the status oracle: 400 no longer distinguishes "unconfigured" from "configured but rejecting our credentials". 018 5 done
    UW-A56 Two designs exist for ADR-018's 16 CFR 312.5© consent record, and the narrower one shipped by default rather than by decision. What is live on main is kws_verification (migration 20260809130000), a KWS-specific table: status set sent/verified/failed, vendor config captured as a single enabled_methods JSONB, one resolution-pairing constraint, and no column naming which verification method produced the row. PR #681 builds on it, adding user.consent_verification_id whose foreign key points at kws_verification(id); the column name therefore promises a generality the referenced table does not have, which is the specific thing that will mislead the next reader. The alternative was authored and never opened as a PR: a method-agnostic consent_verification table carrying method constrained to typed_name_legacy/kws_pv/stripe_card, a four-value status adding pending and expired, and the evidence columns a consent record actually needs under 312.5© (method_config_snapshot, config_version, policy_version, evidence), plus two constraints the shipped table has no equivalent of: completion pairing, and snapshot-required-when-verified. Nothing is broken today and nothing is blocked; the cost lands the moment a second verification method is added, because the shipped shape has no column to distinguish one method's rows from another's and no place to record the configuration that produced a given verification. To decide: whether kws_verification is the permanent model, whether the generalized table replaces it, or whether the shipped table is widened in place; and, if either of the latter, the shipped consent_verification_id column name and its FK target must be reconciled at the same time. Two mechanical constraints for whoever rules: the parked migration is stamped 20260808000000, which sorts before four migrations already applied on main, so adopting it as authored means an out-of-order migration and it must be restamped; and the parked branch is local-only, preserved at tag archive/consent-verification-record (f45c91be) rather than on a remote. Owner to rule: the project owner, jointly with whoever holds the privacy-compliance role, alongside the still-open VPC method decision that UW-N02 and UW-A52 already track, since the method set that decision produces is exactly what the method column would have to enumerate. 018 7 decision
    UW-A57 ADR-030 awaits owner ratification, and the C3 engagement-correlation analysis job is gated on it. The ADR was drafted by an agent under the owner's standing "proceed under assumed approval" ruling for this blocked item, with the mitigation that the job ships flag-OFF behind ANALYSIS_ENGAGEMENT_CORRELATION_ENABLED. It is not a completed human privacy review. Owner who must rule: core-maintainer, on three things specifically: (a) whether the minimum-cohort threshold of 5 distinct family_id values is accepted, since the owner is the only person who may ever lower it, and with it the revision of 2026-08-29 that binds the same floor to each emitted signal's own contributing families rather than only to the reader cohort; (b) whether the never-commit-to-this-public-repository decision on the output artifact stands, now generalised so that the artifact and any excerpt of it does not leave the deployment host by any path, quoting a row into an issue, a PR body, a doc, or an LLM prompt included; © ratification of the no-route constraint. The ADR was revised before ratification following a senior review; the document to ratify is the current one, not the 2026-08-28 draft. Until ratified the flag stays off and no artifact is produced against real reader data; turning it on beforehand voids the mitigation the assumed-approval ruling depends on. 030 5 decision
    UW-A58 ADR-031 awaits owner ratification, and task D3b (the leg C friction beacon) is blocked on it. The plan's D3 entry states "No code before the ADR is accepted", so until this is ratified no beacon client, no POST /api/v1/client-events route, no client_friction_event table, and no digest job may be built. The ADR was drafted by an agent as task D3a and is not a completed human privacy review. Owner who must rule: core-maintainer, on four things specifically: (a) that the kid surface emits nothing in version 1, taken as a categorical exclusion because a child session token names one child profile in a signed claim (api/deps.py:738), which is ADR-030's exclusion of records about one identified subject reached through the session rather than a column; (b) the two dropped variants, offline-sync failure counts (it needs a profile identifier to be actionable, which the plan's own #VERIFY says is grounds for dropping) and dead clicks (its detector requires document-wide DOM observation and a fetch wrapper, the capability the Session Replay prohibition forbids); © the 5-distinct-session publish floor, which matches ADR-030's number on a deliberately weaker unit than its distinct-family count, accepted only on three named compensations that can each individually stop being true; (d) the standing Session Replay prohibition stated as a capability rather than a vendor list, which is what makes it reach first-party reimplementations. Two findings the ADR records that constrain D3b regardless of the ruling: the plan's server leg is not implementable as written, since pipeline_event is trigger-enforced append-only (20260710000000_baseline.sql:524) and cannot carry a retention window, so the beacon needs its own table; and the retention sweep is specified with no exemption set at all, citing UW-C227's sweep-time-versus-decision-time inversion, with the form of any future exemption pre-bound. Depends on ADR-030's ratification only for consistency of the shared floor, not for sequencing. 031 5 decision

    Cluster C: authoring-lessons phase linkage¶

    The authoring lessons log is mandated by CLAUDE.md and has 24 open lessons, none referenced by either master document. Its only plan linkage is one sentence inside capability-register row A11, which the roadmap files under "Post-launch backlog". That is the wrong home for at least two blocking items.

    AL-* is the canonical namespace for the authoring, ceiling-scale-review, and series-stress-test findings; reviews/ceiling-scale-review-2026-07-25.md and series-stress-test-findings.md describe substantially the same work under different headings. Do not triple-book.

    ID Item Phase Status
    UW-C01 AL-014 blocking: no hand-authored skeleton could pass check_promotion_bundle, because the lineage sidecar was unconditional. Still open: AL-014's proposal also asked for an origin sidecar and for the WS-5 anti-clone floor to still apply to a hand-authored original. The floor is parent-relative and cannot run without a lineage parent, so proving an original against its in-cell siblings (the UW-C06 check_incell_clones path) is the remaining work. now unscheduled
    UW-C02 AL-036 blocking: the review surface cannot deliver the human approval ADR-005 requires at 746 nodes (no pagination, virtualization, or per-node state).     UW-C23 ADR-011 section 10's flowed-band choiceless rule ("1, prefer 0" at 8-11, "0-1" above) counts STOPS and is unimplemented. validator/choice_grammar.py's CG-1 caps consecutive single-choice NODES at 6 for those bands, which is a derived words-per-stop backstop, not the ADR rule, and its #ASSUME now says so. Implementing the real rule needs stop-level adjacency (ADR-026 compose_stop boundaries), which nothing in the validator computes today. Surfaced by the PR #532 review (I8). 4b unscheduled
    UW-C24 Every CG-1..CG-4 choice-grammar rule is inert in production: validator/gate.py::run_gate defaults enforce_grammar=False and no production caller passes True (only tests/unit/test_choice_grammar.py does). The D3/D11 grandfathering rationale is sound, but the flip condition was recorded nowhere. It is now stated in the module docstring: the flag flips when the D11 deprecated per-skeleton marker lands (W2.4), at which point the gate can enforce for unmarked (new) skeletons and skip marked ones, and the default can become True. Until then the rules produce no finding on any real story. Surfaced by the PR #532 review (I9). Ruled 2026-08-05 (D21): keep False; flip in the same PR as the D11 deprecated marker (W2.4). Flipping earlier fails the entire grandfathered catalog, since every current skeleton predates the rules. Open prerequisite, and the reason this is blocked rather than unscheduled: the D11 deprecated per-skeleton marker (W2.4) does not exist yet, and the ruling ties the flag flip to the same PR that lands it. Second consequence, AL-577 (2026-08-23): because the default is False, a measurement taken through a bare run_gate(doc) silently omits every CG-* finding and looks like a valid result. Re-measuring check_skeleton.py's advisory incidence that way returned 35 of 84 shells FIRING a blocking advisory, every one of them with zero CG-* findings, which is not a statement that 35 shells are grammar-clean: under that flag no shell can produce a CG-* finding at all; through the script's own load_skeleton(..., enforce_grammar=True) path it is 64 of 84, dominated by CG-3 (64), CG-2 (54) and CG-1 (41). Until the flag flips, any claim about catalog gate outcomes has to name which oracle produced it. 4b blocked

    UW-C26 AL-082 BandProfile.reconvergence_ceiling is declared, defaults to None, is set on no band, and is read only by the offline mutation/ layer; no validator rule consults it, so the request path has no reconvergence constraint while the field implies one.     UW-C33 AL-100 The frontend format gate is scoped to src/**, not the tree: format:check runs prettier --check "src/**/*.{ts,tsx,css,json}" (frontend/package.json), so e2e/, e2e-real/, eslint.config.js, index.html, and README.md are unchecked, and npx prettier --check . reports 130 unformatted files under frontend/. This is the TypeScript twin of the Python gap PR #635 closed with the format-tree job, which deliberately does not cover it (ruff does not format TypeScript). The gap has TWO loci, not one: the pre-commit hook frontend-prettier is scoped the same way (files: ^frontend/src/ plus the same four extensions), so staging an e2e-real spec reports "no files to check" and passes. Fixing only the npm script leaves the hook blind. Widen both, honouring .prettierignore (which already exempts src/client/, dist/, coverage/, playwright-report/, test-results/), and land the resulting mechanical reformat as its own commit so it is reviewable as formatting rather than mixed into a behaviour change. Two corrections to this row, both measured 2026-08-24 in a clean worktree off origin/main at b97c7cbf. (a) Both counts are correct, and their disagreement is itself the finding. 53 is what a clean checkout reports; 130 is what any checkout that has run design-sync reports, because the balance are generated files under frontend/design-system/ (ds-bundle/, .ds-sync/, .design-sync/.cache/). Those are gitignored, but by frontend/design-system/.gitignore, and Prettier's default --ignore-path is ['.gitignore', '.prettierignore'] resolved against the CWD only: it never reads a nested .gitignore. The original 130 was therefore not a dirty checkout in the sense of uncommitted edits, and dismissing it as unreproducible was wrong. Measured both ways on 2026-08-24 during the PR #754 review: 0 unformatted in a fresh worktree, 78 on the same commit with the design-sync artefacts materialised. Zero of the 53 tracked files are under src/, which is the expected shape: the old gate already held src/ clean, so the unformatted set is exactly the set the gate could not see. (b) The row's list of missed paths is incomplete in the way that matters most. Beyond e2e/, e2e-real/, eslint.config.js, index.html and README.md, 26 of the 53 are in frontend/design-system/, a separate npm package (@cyo/design-system) reached by the app through the @ds alias in vite.config.ts and tsconfig.app.json. It is not a workspace of frontend/package.json, so no glob rooted there ever reached it. Its own CI job (ci.yml:357) runs typecheck, test and build and has no lint or format step at all, which makes it a second, wider scope hole than the one this row was opened for: see the companion lint finding, since frontend/package.json's lint script does not name it either and the design-system package has no lint script, so its 44 files and 1,292 lines are linted by nothing in CI. Fixed on unpushed branch feat/frontend-assurance-gaps, two commits as this row asks: d39a8a8d widens both loci (npm scripts to prettier --check ., hook files: to ^frontend/.* over Prettier-supported extensions only, since the hook passes explicit paths and Prettier exits non-zero rather than skipping on an unparseable one like nginx.conf), and 471c7acd carries the 53-file reformat alone. Exclusions were left to .prettierignore rather than duplicated into the glob, which works because Prettier honours that file even for explicitly-passed paths; package-lock.json was added to it on the existing src/client/ rule that generated output is formatted by its generator. That exclusion set was initially incomplete, per (a): it named only the artefacts frontend/.prettierignore could see, so format:check was green in CI and red on any machine that had run design-sync, and npm run format would have rewritten ~2.6 MB of generated output including the 1,111,175-byte vendored design-system/ds-bundle/_vendor/react.js. The four design-sync output directories were added to frontend/.prettierignore in the PR #754 review pass and the fix was verified 78 to 0 on the PR branch with the artefacts present. Discrimination was proven rather than assumed: the widened hook fails on frontend/e2e-real/admin-management-real.spec.ts, a path the old ^frontend/src/ pattern provably never matched, so it is not passing on an empty file list. Note the reformat is not whitespace-only, because .prettierrc sets semi: false and singleQuote: true. Verified green after it: frontend lint (unchanged single pre-existing warning), tsc -b, 2384 vitest tests, npm run build with the check-sw-denylist postbuild guard, and the design-system package's own typecheck, 72 tests, build and react-stack-top-frame bundle guard. Status stays unscheduled on the UW-C74 precedent: the branch is unmerged and unpushed, so the gate on main is still the narrow one. Closes when that branch merges. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C61 AL-126 Running pre-commit while other agents hold uncommitted, unstaged edits in the same shared worktree is unsafe: pre-commit stashes unstaged changes for the hook run and restores them afterward, and a concurrent agent's write landing inside that window can be silently reverted by the restore, reported only as a bland "Restored changes from ...patch..." line. This is a distinct hazard from the "no concurrent git writes" rule that protects the index; it fires even when every agent in the worktree obeys that rule. Propose: document in the parallel-dispatch coordination pattern that pre-commit must not run while any other agent in the same worktree may still be mid-write; batch commits after every in-flight agent has reported, against a quiescent tree. now unscheduled
    UW-C62 AL-127 Introducing a dedicated build node to set a character's archetype, fed by all six archetype-setting choices, silently disqualifies the sorting_hat topology: validator/topology.py's admissibility check disqualifies any node with in-degree >= 2, and a six-choice build node has in-degree 6 by construction. Caught only by reading topology.py before editing; a failing gate run would never have surfaced it, since the disqualification happens at topology classification, not inside the CH-* rules. The working alternative (Task 11): attach each archetype-setting effect to a choice that already exists on the tree's own branch points, adding no new node and no new converging edge. Propose: document the trap and the working pattern in .claude/skills/cyo-author/reference/skeleton-format.md's "The archetype build node" section, alongside the gate-node/build-node idiom already documented there. (docs/architecture/story-skeletons.md, which an earlier draft of this row named, has no build-node section.) now unscheduled

    UW-C64 AL-129 A stat-envelope book's immutable might/wits/nerve cannot gate a choice condition directly on a stat threshold without tripping a blocking L2-11 dead-branch error, because validator/layer2.py::_check_dead_branches walks only the baseline declared-initial configuration with zero accepts_character awareness; the archetype variable's build-node idiom (design spec section 4.3) does not transfer, since stat books deliberately have no in-story mutating node. Task 12 found a working authoring-only fix (an auxiliary non-canonical resolve boolean, reset by an unconditioned choice immediately before the gate and XOR-combined with the stat threshold, verified to add zero per-walk configuration cost across all 27 envelope states) but made no validator change. The workaround is not a solution, and the pilot proved it. The XOR pairing needs a free player choice able to flip the outcome immediately before every gate, so the reader cannot perceive their stat deciding anything; applied across a whole book it made all 14 stations mechanically identical. The 13-16 pilot skeleton built on it was withdrawn from the branch on 2026-08-08 and preserved out of tree rather than shipped, which also leaves UW-A46's pathfinder pilot open. Documented (this branch, uncommitted): the trap and the pattern, including its perceptibility cost, are now in the two docs an author reads, .claude/skills/cyo-author/reference/skeleton-format.md ("The stat-gate wall") and docs/architecture/story-skeletons.md; the design spec was the wrong home, since authors do not read it. The open work this row now carries is a decision, not the doc fix: whether _check_dead_branches should become envelope-aware, quantifying reachability over the entry states accepts_character declares rather than the baseline configuration alone. That is what would make a genuine stat gamebook authorable without the workaround, but it changes a rule every catalog skeleton is already gated by, so the blast radius is catalog-wide and it needs a measured re-gate of the whole catalog first. Not decided, and not to be recorded as decided; the question should be answered before another stat-envelope pilot is attempted. now decision

    UW-C66 Found 2026-08-08 by the final whole-branch review of PR for feat/persistent-characters-runtime (review triage Verdict B, item B11).     UW-C69 Found 2026-08-08 by the final whole-branch review of PR for feat/persistent-characters-runtime (review triage Verdict B, item B15). That review report is not tracked in git, so the durable anchors for this row are the file paths and symbol names cited below. CharacterName (api/schemas.py:1632, Annotated[str, Field(min_length=1, max_length=32), AfterValidator(_nfc)]) applies the max_length=32 bound before AfterValidator(_nfc) NFC-normalizes the value, and db/models.py:944's name column is VARCHAR(32). Because NFC normalization can change a string's codepoint count, an input that passes the pre-normalization length check can still normalize to a value that overflows the column, which would surface as an unhandled database error rather than the 422 the length bound exists to give. Reorder to normalize before bounding length (or re-check length after normalization) so the 422 path is the one that actually fires. Exotic input, but a child-authored free-text field reaching a raw column-width failure is a 500-class defect worth tracking even at low likelihood. 5 unscheduled

    UW-C73 AL-131 The CH-* family proves a character envelope is declared and survivable but never that it is observable, so a book can pass all eight rules with an envelope that changes nothing a reader can perceive. Demonstrated twice: the-storm-chasers-club's archetype gate was a tautology (its only inbound edge set the value the gate tested, true across all seven entry states) and the withdrawn 13-16 stat pilot was imperceptible via AL-129's XOR workaround. Both passed the gate; both promotions were withdrawn. The reachability rules are silent by construction, since a tautological gate is maximally reachable. Proposed: a rule failing any character-variable condition whose truth is invariant across the declared envelope, plus a rule requiring at least one condition whose truth varies across the declared entry states, both computable from the per-state walk validator/character.py already performs.     UW-C105 Canonical for the sibling-fill gate-revise-regate loop; UW-C147 is the same finding under AL-215 and cross-references this row. AL-169 The gate-revise-regate fill loop closed a 20.4 to 1.2 per-1000 gram gap in one agentic round with no human repair. Original ask: wire check_sibling_fills (plus title and menu checks) into the generation fill loop as a blocking gate with an automated revision pass, capped rounds before human flag. Partly satisfied 2026-08-23, and the residual is narrower than the original ask. The anti-template guard now runs inside the moderation pipeline (moderation/leaf_diversity.py, wired at moderation/pipeline.py), emitting one Verdict.FLAG per templated node on FAIL, which routes the book into the pipeline's existing bounded repair and then to the human gate; self-repetition and narrative-person drift ship alongside it as Verdict.ADVISORY (moderation/prose_craft.py). Three things the original ask names are still missing, and each is a separate reason this cannot be closed. (1) It is not a blocking gate: the ATG is advisory-plus-soft-flag by contract and never rejects. (2) There is one repair round, not capped rounds: the pipeline's single bounded repair is not a revise-regate loop, and adding rounds means re-running the gate after each, which nothing does today. (3) The menu-frame check (check_sibling_fills.py::menu_frame_overlap, the recognition channel AL-161 identified) has no request-path counterpart at all. A fourth item closed 2026-08-23: the sibling-gram measure reached the request path when #742 merged as 37a08a60, so diversity/grams.py is now the one definition moderation/leaf_diversity.py and scripts/check_sibling_fills.py both read; they differ only in include_choice_labels, which is a deliberate difference of question rather than drift. Promotion of any of the advisories to a gate additionally needs a threshold distribution measured across real books, which no run has produced; picking a cut-off without one is how an advisory becomes a false-reject engine. Depends on UW-C315's unresolved lever decision for what a blocking threshold could even be set to. now unscheduled
    UW-C106 AL-170 With devices, prose, menus, and titles varied, recognition still lands at node 4 via choice-menu semantics and scene-function order: sibling fills of one skeleton are structurally a series. Owner decision: series framing and an at-most-one-binding-per-reader assignment policy, and/or per-fork semantics pools rotated at selection time; catalog diversity comes from more skeletons. now unscheduled

    UW-C111 AL-175 The prose-craft detector thresholds were tuned on the same nine books they are reported to separate, so the perfect separation is in-sample fit rather than predictive power, and two thresholds are already known fragile (told-emotion ranks one unpublishable book below a shippable one; the moral-tag budget of zero is unfalsifiable on this corpus). Validate against a held-out corpus from a different skeleton and band, re-tune on the union, and keep the detectors advisory-only until then. now unscheduled
    UW-C112 AL-176 Anti-repeat history is family-scoped with a 20-row window and no per-child skeleton history exists, which triples the second-request repeat rate at three children and hides 81% of a child's own band reading. Recovers 22% of required catalog at the N=25 target and 46% at N=10 for no new content (corrected 2026-08-09 from a stated 25 to 30%, which matched neither endpoint). now unscheduled
    UW-C113 AL-177 Catalog scales with per-child lifetime consumption, not user count, so catalog build is bounded capital: about 106 skeletons at 0.5 stories per child per month, 204 at 1, 408 at 2, 816 at 4, against 57 today (corrected 2026-08-09; the earlier "130 at 0.5, 408 at 1, 816 at 2" was shifted by one rate step and quoted 130 rather than 106 at 0.5).     UW-C114 AL-178 Every populated cell holds 3 to 5 skeletons (median 4, recounted 2026-08-22), so a child exhausts a cell by their 4th or 5th request and repeats become certain.     UW-C115 AL-179 SAFE-14 is an empty stub so safety_flagged is a constant, yet it was reported as safety evidence seven times; the real pre-LLM denylist floor runs on the theme-binding path only.     UW-C116 AL-180 The strict authoring bar passes 2 of 61 catalog skeletons (0 of 11 at 10-13) and the pilots' control skeleton is a non-production-eligible off-matrix seed the selector never serves. Decide whether the strict bar is the production bar and re-baseline diversity work on a production-eligible skeleton in a real cell. Re-measured 2026-08-22 by running check_skeleton.py over all 84 shells with and without --strict: 20 of 84 pass strict, not 2 of 61, so the cost is retiring 74% of the production-eligible catalog (55 of 74), not 97%. Two findings sharpen the decision. First, the gate that promotion actually runs is not in question: only 3 of 84 shells fail without --strict, and all three are already production_eligible: false, so nothing a request can reach fails today. Second, the delta is entirely the CG-* grammar family, which --strict promotes from advisory to blocking: of the 55 eligible shells strict would reject, 21 fail on CG-* alone and 34 fail on CG-* plus something structural, while zero fail on structural grounds alone (CG-3 accounts for 1,557 of the findings, almost all of it the words-per-stop ceiling). So the question to rule on is whether CG-* is calibrated for the catalog we ship, not whether the structural floors are right. now unscheduled
    UW-C117 AL-181 Length demand concentrates on medium while the catalog is flat at 3 to 4 per cell, and cells are hard partitions (0 of 58 production skeletons carry a wildcard length), so short and long surplus is stranded and the medium cell binds in every band. Author against the demand curve: first 6 to 12 skeletons per band go entirely to its medium cell, and measure the real length distribution to replace the assumed regimes. now unscheduled
    UW-C118 AL-182 Kid auto-approve hardcodes length=SHORT for every band, steering unchosen requests into the thinnest-demand tier and producing a guaranteed 422 for teen-band children because 13-16 and 16+ have no short cell by design; ADR-011 band-by-length is also unvalidated at intake so a long 3-5 or short 13-16 request fails late. Make auto-approve length band-aware and demand-weighted, add band-by-length validation to the approve and authored-create bodies, and gate the frontend length options by band. now unscheduled

    UW-C233 AL-327 A fill that returns no parseable document seeds the repair loop with the unfilled skeleton (orchestrator.py:1000, :1013); repair makes no progress, the loop stops after one attempt, and the skeleton is returned as the storybook.     UW-C259 AL-389 validator/reading_level.py::_count_syllables over-counts every regular -ed past by one syllable (walked 2, opened 3), inflating Flesch-Kincaid by a measured 0.16 to 0.55 grades on committed books, largest at 0.55 on a 5-8 book against a 3-5 tolerance of only ±1.0.     UW-C263 AL-396 Nothing in the pipeline checks a node against the path that reached it, so a book that declares no state still asserts accumulated history. the-tide-pool-rescue declares variables: [] and its endings name 3 or 4 animals on paths where a reader can have met 1, with e_tide_snail claiming "Three rescues by you" on a ten-node path containing one; two further instances hold in baking-day-with-grandma-vole and the-sleepy-little-star. No L1, L2, PL, RL, CH or CG rule asks the question and no judge criterion does either, since the panel scores one node's prose rather than its consistency with its own history. Build it in two tiers, scoped as named-entity continuity and not as information-state coverage: (1) an ending or merge node asserting a count or a set requires declared variables that can carry it, exact and decidable from metadata alone; (2) every path to a definite reference must pass through a node introducing that named entity, using validator/paths.py for enumeration and string matching for first mention. W15 is not a precedent against this because W15 failed on entailment (AL-366) and both tiers refuse that problem; the limit both miss (state established as "she tucked it away", asserted as "safe in her pocket") must be stated in the rule rather than papered over. Tier 2 per-reference checking yields 3.48 findings per node; the lexical Tier 1 scores 1 true positive in 6; the exact structural version (ancestors minus dominators) flags 3,815 of 4,472 nodes, correctly, because that is what branching means. The measure shipped as a reported statistic in validator/continuity.py, not as a rule. What remains is a catalogue decision, not a checker: all six loop_and_grow skeletons declare no variables, no on_enter and no conditional choices while carrying 2 to 14 multi-history nodes, three of them with verified continuity defects, and the schema forbids their Tier 1 from declaring variables at all. CORRECTED 2026-08-15 (AL-409): do NOT reject loop_and_grow at Tier 1. ADR-011's per-band allowance table lists it for both 3-5 and 5-8 and states that their loops are "the non-stateful try-again kind, legal at Tier 1", distinct from stateful progress loops which first apply at 8-11. The six skeletons conform; the proposed gate would have rejected the ADR's own design. The real defect is that the SAME ADR gives loop_and_grow the purpose "state growth per loop" and the topology is named for growth, so an author at 3-5 is told to grow something and denied the mechanism to represent it. Amend ADR-011 so the purpose column reads try-again at 3-5 and 5-8, and carry that distinction onto the skeletons where an author will see it. Done 2026-08-15 (AL-411): ADR-011's topology table now splits the purpose column by band and carries a per-band amendment stating that at 3-5 and 5-8 nothing may be counted, collected or referred back to as already-met, repeated in drafting-guide.md and cyo-author/SKILL.md step 3a where the author is at fill time.     UW-C264 AL-397 Every safety check in the pipeline scores what happens TO a character and none scores what a child is invited to imitate, so a book that rewards an imitable hazard passes cleanly: content_flags, the L1 safety rules and the moderation classifiers all ask whether anything bad happens, and in a book of this kind nothing does. Two independent readers named the class on different book sets, citing an enclosed snow tunnel crawled repeatedly as the "Greatest fort ever" ending (the-snow-day-expedition i_f2/e_i_tunnel), a bravery payoff for lighting a wick solo (the-night-market e_light_pip), and opening a hot oven offered as a choice whose cost is slumped buns (baking-day-with-grandma-vole n_peek_oops/n_funny_peek). The gap survives, because depicted supervision is a mitigation nothing in the pipeline asked for or could have detected. Add an imitable_practice judge criterion scoring whether a guardian would accept a child imitating what the protagonist is REWARDED for, with in-text mitigation as the thing that moves the score, routed by a short deterministic keyword screen. Validate it through W7 before it arbitrates, like every other criterion. Screen half done 2026-08-15 (AL-405): validator/imitable.py routes 13 of 167 young-band endings to human attention and catches all three endings the readers named, scoped to endings only (what the book rewards), young bands only (ungated the flame cue fires on 23 endings, mostly 16+), and co-occurrence rather than single words (climb alone matched 10.8 percent of endings). Six of the thirteen are one lantern-festival book, the correct failure mode for a router. The judged imitable_practice criterion remains unbuilt and must not arbitrate before W7 shows it detects a seeded unmitigated-hazard arm. now unscheduled
    UW-C265 AL-398 A reader review's converged pattern and its individual instances have very different reliability, and the current brief does not distinguish them. Two Fable readers on different book sets with no contact named the same five classes, which is strong evidence the classes are real; of the four instances checked, one was refuted outright (all 15 paths to the-night-market's f_string, e_up_chorus, f_lantern and f_friends DO pass through the node establishing the wish), two were overstated on the supervision facts, and one was exactly right and provable from metadata. The cause is structural rather than a bad reader: a reader reasons over the text in front of it and cannot enumerate paths, so it is reliable about what a book does and unreliable about what every route through it does. Write the verification step into the blind-spot review brief: every instance carries a node id and a quote so it can be checked, path claims are checked with validator/paths.py rather than by re-reading, and no instance reaches a register row or a ticket unverified. Done 2026-08-15: the brief did not exist as a document (it had been written inline into each reader's prompt), so it now does, at docs/planning/blind-spot-review-brief.md, carrying the four checked instances and their outcomes, the structural reason a reader is reliable about what a book does and unreliable about what every route through it does, and the rule that a path claim is checked with validator/paths.py rather than by re-reading. now done

    UW-C271 FallbackProvider has no single answer to 'which model serves this call', so a cascade's fallback legs can run under an output cap resolved for its primary. Cheap to implement once decided, roughly four small files, and it cannot be inferred from the code because it is a semantics call about what a cascade IS. 3 unscheduled

    UW-C273 AL-444: TAU_STRUCT is derived as the 25th percentile of same-cell pairwise structural distance across the committed catalog, so every skeleton authored moves it, and it can move DOWN.     UW-C275 AL-447: the story-first experiment. A strong model given the 5-8 age rules and NO skeleton produced a markedly good story with a structure the gate rejects, and the rejection is almost entirely a units mismatch rather than a defect: 16 passages at a 253-word median against a 155-word per-node ceiling, and a 5-node fastest arc against a floor of 7. Re-cut at the band's own 70-word mean the identical 3,944 words imply about 56 nodes at roughly depth 18, inside 5-8/medium's budget and past its arc floor. Two things to decide. First, whether to document the unit (a node is a rendered stop, not a scene) in the drafting guide and skeleton brief, and whether to build the decomposition pass that would make a story-first draft a legitimate input, which is worth weighing because a story-first draft is measurably stronger on exactly the qualities no gate measures. Second, and larger: the story is a pure tree with zero merges, and the writer explicitly rejected funnelling paths into a shared scene as 'cheap'. The strict bar makes reconvergence effectively compulsory above 3-5 (UW-C272), so every ending behind a bottleneck is one some reader reaches without having earned it on their own path, which is the defect class AL-443 and AL-448 keep finding by hand. Whether that is an acceptable cost of the node budget is an owner call, not a validator one. Superseded in part by AL-453, the same experiment repeated at 8-11: that story reconverges five times unprompted and the writer justified each merge, so the convergence conflict was an artefact of the 5-8 story's LENGTH rather than of craft, and this half of the row should not drive work. What replaces it is larger: at 8-11 every passage is one rendered stop, and 0 of 19 fit the 135-word CG-3 ceiling, median 439, against the architecture-first skeleton in the same cell whose 61 stops all fit at a median of 100. That is a 4.4x disagreement about how often a 9-year-old should meet a choice, it cannot be re-cut away at a flowed band, and it is a calibration question about the ceiling rather than an authoring defect. Third, and already half-answered elsewhere: the re-cut draft reads as 11 discrete pages at 5-8, with 29 no-decision nodes each costing a tap, because ADR-026 decision 4 rules that 3-5 and 5-8 render one node per page while FLOWED_BANDS in frontend/src/reader/readerProgress.ts flows 8-11 and up into scrollable rendered stops. Beat-granular authoring and continuous reading are therefore already compatible at 8-11 and above and are NOT at 5-8. ADR-026 considered and rejected flowing at every band (Alternative 3) on the argument that comprehension competes with interaction for pre-readers; note that argument is much stronger at 3-5 than at 5-8, so extending flow to 5-8 alone is a narrower change than the one rejected and would make a story-first 5-8 draft authorable at beat length and readable as continuous prose. That is an ADR-026 amendment rather than a code change, though the code side is small: the Python compose_stop has no production caller, so the reader's flow is entirely the TypeScript implementation plus its conformance corpus. 5 decision
    UW-C276 AL-452, AL-454: CG-3 has a hole that inverts its own incentive. It measures only a composed stop that contains at least one no-decision node, because _find_runs yields runs of single-choice nodes and a decision node with nothing in front of it heads no run. Measured at 8-11: one decision node of 200 words draws zero findings, while the same material as a 70-word no-decision node plus a 70-word decision, 60 words fewer in total, fires CG-3. Since PL-19's per-node max at 8-11 is 220 against a stop ceiling of 135, up to 220 words of rendered stop go unmeasured, and an author can silence the rule by merging nodes together, which is the opposite of what it wants. Fix is small (treat a decision or ending node with no preceding run as a stop of one) but it will fire on committed content, so it needs the CG family's grandfathering rather than a silent tightening. Carries a THIRD half from AL-454: ADR-011 section 10 rules a words-per-stop range for all six bands (10-40 at 3-5, 30-70 at 5-8, 60-135 at 8-11, 80-150 at 10-13, 100-200 at 13-16, 100-230 at 16+, adopted as D15 with a cited research appendix), and _WORDS_PER_STOP_CEILING implements only the four flowed bands. At 3-5 and 5-8 a node is a stop, so the unenforced range is instead governed by words_per_node_profile, which is about twice as permissive at the top (55 and 95 advisory, 90 and 155 hard, against 40 and 70 researched). Give CG-3 the two missing entries from the ADR's own numbers and reconcile the two tables, then re-check the committed catalog before treating the result as a bar; the re-cut 5-8 story-first draft passes strict today with 37 of 44 stops outside the researched range. Carries a second, documentation half: AL-447 concluded that a story-first draft is fixed by decomposing scenes into beats, which is correct at 3-5 and 5-8 where ADR-026 renders one node per page, and wrong at 8-11 and up where the nodes re-compose into one stop; at a flowed band the binding constraint is that prose between two decisions fits the stop ceiling, which is an authorial property rather than a bookkeeping one. DONE, verified against the tree 2026-08-18 during the stale-row reconciliation. choice_grammar.py::_check_standalone_stops treats a decision or ending node with no preceding run as a stop of one, and 3-5 and 5-8 gained the ADR's own words-per-stop ceilings.     UW-C283 AL-458 lanes 1 and 3 INDEPENDENTLY: PL-17's flat 15 percent endings floor contradicts ADR-011 section 5's own per-cell endings column. _ENDINGS_FRACTION = 0.15 applied to node count demands more endings than the ADR's cell states for the same node count: 10-13/long at the ADR's top of range (340 nodes, 48 endings) fails "below scale-adjusted minimum 51"; 10-13/medium 32 against 33; 3-5/medium 6 against 7. Three cells are unbuildable at the top of their own stated node envelope and eleven more lose part of their stated range (16+/long loses 16 of 25 values). The two ADR statements disagree with each other and the code implements section 6, so the gate contradicts section 5. Two lanes reaching this from different directions is the reason it is filed separately from UW-C277. Now carries a measured craft cost from AL-457: the 13-16 story-first draft re-cut to 118 nodes fails only PL-17 (18 endings wanted against 14) and PL-20, while its author reported that 14 endings at 200 to 350 words each already took about a third of the budget and compressed mid-branch scenes toward summary. So the floor asks for four more of the most expensive structure in the story. Decide which ADR section is authoritative, and whether the floor should scale on node count at all at the teen bands. Related but distinct: PL-17 counts buttons rather than forks (len(node.choices) >= 2, so two choices aimed at one target count as a decision), affecting 40 catalog nodes. Owner framing 2026-08-18 (not yet a ruling on the number): ending count is a four-way balance, and any proposal has to argue all four. Too many endings and paths become individual, which is the reconvergence cost from the other side. Too many UNSUCCESSFUL endings and a reader cannot find the positive one. Too few endings and unnecessary reconvergence is forced. And the constraint no rule currently encodes: in a series, all positive endings must converge to a single starting point for the next book, so a high positive-ending count makes that handoff harder. That last one is new to this row and cuts against simply lowering the floor: it bounds POSITIVE endings specifically, where PL-17 floors endings in aggregate and PL-24 ceilings the dominant KIND share, so neither rule currently expresses it. Any change here should be modelled against the series handoff (validator/series.py SR-9 exit-state enumeration) before it is set, since a book that satisfies PL-17 by adding positive endings makes its own sequel harder to author. DONE, verified against the tree 2026-08-18 during the stale-row reconciliation. _CELL_ENDING_BOUNDS carries ADR-011 section 5's per-cell endings column and breadth_scaled_floors caps the scaled floor by the cell's own stated maximum, so the floor can no longer exceed the ceiling. 5 done

    UW-C287 AL-462: PL-26's prose and gamebook ceilings now CROSS, because one is anchor-derived and the other is not.
