    UW-C289 AL-465: CG-4 and reconvergence are in direct structural conflict, and CG-4 only became capable of firing on 2026-08-18. CG-4 requires a decision-child's opening prose to share a content word with the choice label that led there. A reconvergent hub has many parents, so its opening must be generic enough to follow ANY incoming choice and cannot acknowledge a specific one. Measured over 12 committed filled books, CG-4 findings per node by in-degree: 1 parent 0.16, 2-3 parents 0.55, 4+ parents 1.56, a tenfold rate increase. Meanwhile UW-C272 establishes that above 3-5 the only achievable topologies are the reconverging ones, so the topology rules push authors toward exactly the shape CG-4 penalises. Two story-first writers reported the craft side independently and unprompted: the 13-16 draft said a merged passage cannot name who is present, and the 16+ draft (whose busiest hub has 15 parents) said it "paid for reconvergence in acknowledgment sharpness". The conflict was invisible until CG-4 started firing. Decide whether CG-4 should scale its expectation with in-degree (a 15-parent hub cannot acknowledge 15 choices and should not be asked to), whether it should apply only to single-parent children, or whether the acknowledgment belongs in the CHOICE LABEL plus first clause rather than the whole opening sentence. Do not simply raise the threshold: the rule is measuring something real at in-degree 1. DONE, verified against the tree 2026-08-18 during the stale-row reconciliation. CG-4 was RESTRICTED rather than loosened: it now fires only on a decision-child of in-degree 1, where a reconvergent target genuinely cannot acknowledge the specific choice that reached it.     UW-C297 AL-473, AL-474: CG-1 and CG-2 count declared choices while PL-20, PL-25 and PL-26 now count visible ones, so a Tier-2 story can score as a compliant 3-way fan and show a child one button. choice_grammar.py::_is_decision and _is_single_choice read len(node.choices) and never look at choice.condition. Measured on the 8-11 series draft before repair: 16 reachable configurations at one node and 2 at another where the reader sees exactly ONE option, on nodes CG-2 grades as compliant 3-way decisions. L2-14 does not cover it either, because it fires only where two or more choices are visible, so the one-visible case is exempt by construction. This is the same condition-blindness UW-C292 fixed for the path rules, left behind in the grammar rules. Needs a tier decision as well as a fix: making CG-1/CG-2 state-aware means walking the configuration graph in the grammar layer, which is the cost UW-C292 already priced at 4.2s per walk at the cap. Measured 2026-08-18, and the exposure is real rather than theoretical. 14 of 68 skeletons declare a choice condition (10-13: 3, 13-16: 6, 16+: 5). 40 nodes declared with 2+ choices show exactly ONE option in some reachable configuration, but most of those are ordinary closed gates and counting them would fire on the feature. The harm is the RUN: the-cinder-bazaar has a chain of ten consecutive one-option stops that CG-1 scores as a longest run of 3 against a cap of 6, verified by walking a real configuration path (a3_ap1_exit through cg3_dry), and the-quiet-harbor-protocol walks 8 against the same cap. Two committed books railroad a reader past CG-1's own cap while CG-1 reports them compliant. Two cost figures I gave earlier were wrong and are corrected here: CG-1/CG-2/CG-3 sit behind enforce_grammar, which no production caller enables (only check_skeleton.py --strict), so this is offline authoring cost and not gate latency; and one walk over the largest conditioned committed book takes 0.169s, not the 4.2s I quoted, which was the 99,423-configuration gamebook draft. Options and recommendation: docs/planning/cg297-condition-blind-grammar-options.md. Recommended C, a new CG-5 measuring the longest visible-run over the configuration graph against the existing _DISCRETE_RUN_CAP, leaving CG-1 and CG-2 unchanged, shipped ADVISORY because all four strict-bar skeletons declare zero conditions and so cannot test the bound. RULED 2026-08-18 (owner): option C, done. New CG-5 measures the longest chain of consecutive one-option configurations against run_cap_for_band, the same per-band value CG-1 applies; CG-1 and CG-2 are unchanged. Solved by longest-path over a topological order of the induced subgraph in O(V+E), not by memoised recursion, which a ~100,000-vertex configuration graph would overflow, and not by path enumeration, which is exponential; a cycle of one-option configurations is reported as its members rather than iterated, leaving unescapable loops to L2-10. Runs only for a story that conditions something and skips a capped walk. Defers to CG-1 whenever CG-1 already fires, so what it adds is only the corridor CG-1 cannot see. Fires on exactly the 2 measured books (the-cinder-bazaar 10 against a declared 3, the-quiet-harbor-protocol 8 against a declared 6), both of which already fail --strict, so the census is unchanged at 4 pass / 66 fail. Advisory with a stated flip condition: blocking once one conditioned book is authored to the strict bar and passes it deliberately. Shipped with the --headroom line the analysis recommended, which prints the declared and visible run side by side plus the hidden excess, so an author sees the gap whether or not the cap is breached. 3 done

Chunking makes the selection screen unnecessary for the path it guards: the largest single node in the catalog is 250 declared words (500 tokens) against a 26,214-token batch budget under the smallest configured model cap, and PL-19's hardest per-node maximum is 230 words, so UnpartitionableSkeletonError is unreachable for any gate-valid skeleton and no skeleton is 'too large for any backend'. A bound fill is one-shot at the RESOLVED cap, which on the shipped default (anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5, ceiling 64,000) is 51,200 tokens or 25,600 declared words, less than half the selection screen. 19 of 82 committed skeletons exceed it, four of them authored yesterday.     UW-C305 AL-477: the TAU_STRUCT drift UW-C273 warned about has now happened and is measured. Adding fourteen skeletons moved the anti-clone floor from 0.304707 to 0.283727, a 6.9 percent WEAKENING in one authoring pass, and scripts/calibrate_mutation_floors.py --check fails until the baseline is recommitted. This is the exact mechanism UW-C273 describes: the calibrator derives TAU_STRUCT as the 25th percentile of same-cell pairwise structural distance across the committed catalog, so authoring meant to strengthen the catalog dilutes the bar that keeps it varied, silently. The recalibrated baseline is NOT committed here, because committing it would resolve UW-C273 by default in the "the floor may fall" direction, which is the owner's call and not mine. Options: adopt the ratchet (TAU_STRUCT may rise, may not fall without an explicit reviewed override), accept the fall as tracking a genuinely more varied catalog, or key the floor to something the catalog cannot move. Until it is ruled, test_calibration_is_deterministic_and_committed_baseline_is_current fails, which is the test doing its job. RULED 2026-08-19 (owner): recalibrate. TAU_STRUCT is now 0.283727, committed. What the recalibration actually shows, measured from the baseline's own stats block rather than from the headline number: same-cell pairs went 91 to 137, and the distance distribution did NOT shift down as a whole. The median is flat (0.379829 to 0.379198, -0.17%) and the maximum ROSE (0.580033 to 0.605791), so the catalog is more varied at the top and unchanged in the middle. What fell is the lower quartile, which is what TAU_STRUCT is defined as: p25 0.304707 to 0.283727 and p05 0.162541 to 0.148890. The minimum is untouched at 0.000469, the pre-existing near-clone pair that TAU_CELL's fixed 0.05 floor already rejects. Every new pair is new-versus-legacy, because the fourteen books went into fourteen distinct cells and no two of them share one, so the reading is that a book authored tightly to a cell's constraints lands closer to whichever legacy member also sits near those constraints than two legacy members do to each other. That is a real property of authoring to a strict bar, not a dilution of the catalog, which is what makes accepting the fall defensible here rather than merely convenient. 3 done

    UW-C345 the same-skeleton reuse cap reads a snapshot, not a reservation, so two concurrent requests from one family can draw the same slug. generation/skeleton_match.py::_apply_reuse_cap filters on recent_usage, which counts existing StorybookVersion rows; build_authoring_plan selects a skeleton before the GenerationJob exists, so a second overlapping request for the same family sees no usage for the first pending job. A one-item cell then repeats deterministically. Accepted for now and documented as an #ASSUME: concurrency block on the function (PR #743): the consequence is one duplicate pairing rather than data loss, the trigger is rare, and serialising the draw would put a family-scoped lock on the request path. reuse_cap_relaxed stays False in that case, so the Selection does not mislabel the duplicate as a known compromise.     UW-C346 D1 is ruled (2026-08-23) and the shipped configuration contradicts it on every leg. The ruling assigns deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro to fill (provisional, to be re-taken before production) and deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash to review, and adds a lane rule the code has no equivalent for: a kid- or guardian-triggered generation may route through OpenRouter or Modal, never through the owner's Anthropic subscription (terms), while admin non-production content may use Sonnet 5 through it. Today no DeepSeek model is a shipped default (core/config.py:461, :462, :567 are all Anthropic), no DeepSeek row is allowlisted (supabase/migrations/20260721230000_seed_provider_model_allowlist.sql seeds four Anthropic rows, two direct-provider), and the allowlist cannot enforce the lane rule because is_enabled_allowlist_pair has one call site, story_requests/authoring_plan.py:274, reached only when plan.mechanism == "automated_provider". generation_provider is a single global Literal (core/config.py:415) with no notion of who triggered the job, so the rule has no enforcement point at all. Proposed: change the leg defaults, add the DeepSeek allowlist rows by migration, and resolve the provider from the requesting actor rather than from a global setting, so a prohibited leg is unreachable under misconfiguration rather than merely undocumented. See D1 in generation-review-workstream-plan-2026-08-22.md. Actor resolution is delivered (commit e03d62e1); the leg defaults and the allowlist migration are implemented on a separate branch, not yet merged. That work surfaced three sub-findings worth recording here. (1) Endpoint pin prerequisite, delivered as background for this work. PRICES in src/cyo_adventure/core/pricing.py is keyed per (provider, model), but OpenRouter serves one slug from many endpoints at different prices, so a row is only true with respect to a named endpoint: the deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro row holds $1.91/$3.83, the price of the pinned azure/us endpoint, while the slug's default route (novita/fp8) is $1.44/$2.88. Promoting the slug to a default without pinning would have made every job's recorded cost_usd roughly 33 percent wrong while cost_complete still read true, so the error would arrive as a confident wrong number rather than a missing one. Resolved by adding an ENDPOINT_PINS table beside PRICES and an endpoint_pin_for() accessor that build_openrouter_leg consults when the caller names no provider order. (2) Reviewer-swap revalidation, open. The Stage-1 batch-size-8 recall parity ratified on 2026-08-01 was measured against the then-current reviewer, Sonnet 4.6, and the current batch prompt; changing review_openrouter_model to deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash invalidates that evidence by the terms the sweep itself recorded, and no test fails to say so. Needs a re-run of scripts/adversarial_harness.py --batch-size against the new reviewer before the 2026-08-01 parity can be treated as current. (3) Output-cap discrepancy, open, low severity. MODEL_OUTPUT_CAPS in src/cyo_adventure/generation/skeleton.py holds one per-slug ceiling of 393,216 for deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro, but declared ceilings vary by endpoint across the slug's routes, from 16,384 (DeepInfra) to 1,048,576 (CoreWeave/Parasail), and the pinned azure/us endpoint declares 384,000. The shipped pin makes this safe today, since the largest one-shot fill asks 131,072, well inside 384,000, but the table's per-slug key cannot express a per-endpoint ceiling, so moving the pin is not a one-line change. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C349 Staging cannot gain a live reviewer without an owner decision, because D1 put both of its legs on the same slug. .env.staging.example pins the fill leg to deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash for cost, and the D1 ruling (2026-08-23) moves review_openrouter_model's default to that same slug. The independence check in moderation/review_provider.py is backend != generator_provider or review_model != generator_model, so the moment anyone sets CYO_ADVENTURE_REVIEW_PROVIDER=openrouter on staging, every book is stamped reviewer_independent=false while a real reviewer is running: the same flag the mock reviewer produces, so the upgrade is invisible in the data it is supposed to improve. It does not fire as the file ships, because review_provider is left at mock there, which is why this is a trap rather than a live defect. Two candidate resolutions, and they trade different things, which is why this is an owner call and not a config edit. (a) Pin staging's FILL leg up to deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro: staging then matches production exactly and the independence check passes honestly, at a higher cost per staging book. (b) Pin the REVIEW leg to a third model: staging stays cheap, but it stops exercising the reviewer production runs, which directly undercuts UW-C347, whose whole point is to re-measure Stage-1 batch parity against the NEW reviewer. Option (b) was drafted during D1 implementation (qwen/qwen3-32b) and reverted unmerged for that reason; the collision analysis survives as a #CRITICAL block in .env.staging.example. Related to UW-C347 (reviewer-swap revalidation) but distinct: that row is about whether the ratified batch size still holds, this one is about staging being able to measure it at all. 4b unscheduled
    UW-C350 The D1 family-lane rule is now enforced at the API write path only; the read path and the table itself still accept a forbidden pair. api/provider_allowlist.py refuses to create or enable a row whose provider is outside FAMILY_LANE_PROVIDERS, which closes the reachable hole (reproduced before the fix: POST returned 201 for anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6, and PUT {enabled: true} returned 200 and re-enabled a row migration 20260823140000 had just disabled). Two tests in tests/integration/test_provider_allowlist_api.py were affected, and one of them, test_db_check_constraints_reject_invalid_values, was proved vacuous by the discovery: it drops ck_provider_model_allowlist_provider and asserts an IntegrityError still arises, which the new family-lane CHECK satisfied on its own, so the test passed while proving nothing about the constraint it names. Both fixed by shaping the row so only the intended constraint can reject it (enabled=False) and naming the cause with match=. The sweep the lesson proposes was run over the changed table and found no further site: provider_model_allowlist_audit carries exactly one CHECK, so its bare raise is unambiguous by construction, and the two sites in tests/integration/test_allowlist_family_lane_constraint.py already assert the constraint name inside the raises block.     UW-C351 The new 422 from the allowlist lane guard reaches the admin as a false explanation. frontend/src/hooks/classifyApiError.ts maps 401, 403, 429, and 5xx explicitly and lets every other status fall through to transient (the comment at that fall-through names 422 as an example, so this is by construction, not an oversight). frontend/src/admin/ProviderAllowlistPage.tsx supplies the transient copy, so an admin refused for naming a forbidden provider is told, on create, "We could not add that entry. Please try again", which invites unlimited retries of an action that can never succeed. The backend already returns an explanatory message in the response body; nothing shows it.     UW-C352 The price-row/endpoint-pin coherence guard only constrains one direction, so a price row measured off a non-default endpoint can ship with no pin and nothing fails. core/pricing.py keeps one ModelPrice per (provider, model), but OpenRouter serves a slug from many endpoints at different prices, so a row is only correct with respect to a named endpoint; ENDPOINT_PINS is what makes the request actually reach the endpoint the row priced. Both existing guards iterate ENDPOINT_PINS: test_every_pinned_model_has_a_price_row asserts set(ENDPOINT_PINS) <= set(PRICES) and test_every_pinned_price_row_names_its_endpoint_in_the_note walks ENDPOINT_PINS.items(). Both therefore prove pin implies price row. Neither proves the converse, and the three PRICES-iterating tests in tests/unit/test_pricing.py check dating, sign, and completeness, never pinning. Add a row whose note names an endpoint and omit the pin, and every guard stays green while the job pays one endpoint and records the other, with cost_complete still reading true. Nothing is mispriced today: one row is pinned and it is pinned correctly, so this is a maintenance hazard on the next row, not a live defect. The concrete way it lands is scripts/refresh_pricing.py, which reads the slug's DEFAULT route from /api/v1/models; the deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro row is deliberately absent from its _WANTED list for exactly this reason, and that exclusion is enforced by nothing but a comment. Wanted: the mirror of the line-348 assertion, scanning PRICES for any row whose note names an endpoint and asserting endpoint_pin_for returns a non-empty order for it, which closes the containment in both directions at zero runtime cost. Source: cross-vendor model panel over the D1 billing path, 2026-08-23; the panel's other two candidate blockers were refuted against source (a pinned call sets allow_fallbacks: False at providers/openrouter.py, so a reroute is a 404 rather than a silent substitution, and the MappingProxyType mutation concern applies identically to every module-level constant). 4b unscheduled
    UW-C353 AL-592 No gate detects a pytest.raises(IntegrityError) assertion that a second CHECK on the same table has quietly made satisfiable for the wrong reason. PostgreSQL reports only the FIRST constraint a row violates, so adding a CHECK to a table converts every pre-existing bare raise against it into an oracle that can pass without the named constraint existing at all. UW-C350(b) hit this for real: test_db_check_constraints_reject_invalid_values drops ck_provider_model_allowlist_provider and asserted a bare IntegrityError, which the new family-lane CHECK then satisfied on its own, so the test passed while proving nothing about the constraint its own docstring names. It was found by adding a match= on suspicion, not by any check. Both instances are fixed and the sweep was run over that table (af476701), so what remains is the general rule, not the instance. Wanted: extend scripts/check_pytest_raises_scope.py, which already parses every pytest.raises block in tests/ for S5778, to also flag a block raising IntegrityError with no match= and no in-block assertion naming a constraint. Scoping note before building it: the cheap version (flag every bare raises(IntegrityError)) has seventeen existing hits across tables nothing has changed, so it needs the grandfathering treatment rad-citation-baseline.toml already uses for RAD citations rather than a flag day; the precise version (flag only tables carrying more than one CHECK) needs the checker to read db/models.py for the per-table CHECK count, which is a real dependency it does not have today. Decide which before writing it. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C355 AL-594 scripts/ is a production-adjacent tree that three of the repository's quality gates do not read. pyproject.toml sets [tool.basedpyright] include = ["src"], [tool.coverage.run] omit lists scripts/*, and sonarcloud.yml passes source-directory: 'src', so no type checker, no coverage measurement, and no SAST covers this directory; the ruff per-file-ignores for scripts/**/*.py additionally drop BLE, the rule family that would have flagged the missing OverflowError arm found in the PR #749 review.     UW-C357 The python-compatibility.yml 3.11 leg is both slow and highly variable, which matters more than either fact alone. Eight samples of the Run tests step, measured at step granularity because job wall clock includes runner setup: 18.6, 26.1, 26.8, 28.6, 29.5, 29.7, 29.8, 31.9 minutes (runs 32428712031 through 32736921879). That is a 13-minute spread around a ~29-minute median, against the 45-minute ceiling PR #749 raised it to, and roughly 3x the 3.14 leg on the same runner. The raise was correct and the evidence is in the sample: the 29.7 and 29.8 entries are cancelled runs, which is how the old 30-minute ceiling reports a kill, and a cancelled run reads as a supersession rather than a timeout. The open question is the variance, not the median. A single 31m54s reading (run 32692322011, quoted in the PR #749 description) is within normal spread and is not by itself evidence of a regression; a leg whose worst case sits 13 minutes above its best has less real headroom than the ceiling suggests.     UW-C369 Two residual coverage gaps in the semgrep-frontend gate, both accepted deliberately when it landed. (a) Semgrep is installed with pip install semgrep==1.157.0, a version pin without a hash, while every uses: in the same workflow is pinned to a commit SHA. A version pin is not a supply-chain equivalent: PyPI yanks and re-uploads are possible and the transitive dependency set is unpinned entirely. Closing it means a hash-pinned requirements file for Semgrep and its full transitive closure, regenerated on every bump, which is why it was not done inline. (b) eslint-plugin-react is absent from frontend/eslint.config.js, so its security-adjacent rules (react/no-danger, react/jsx-no-target-blank) are not armed; the in-repo Semgrep rule cyo-react-dangerously-set-inner-html covers the first and nothing covers the second.     UW-C376 AL-623: a fail-closed union member can be made unrepresentable against if not x, but not against bool(x), x or y or x and y, and no gate sees the difference. PR #760 gave the personalizable-slot tri-state's fail-closed arm its own marker type whose __bool__ returns NoReturn, which makes BasedPyright reject if, if not, while, assert, ternary conditions and comprehension guards over the union. Probed and confirmed NOT rejected: bool(x), x or y, x and y, any([x]); each type-checks clean and raises TypeError only at runtime. Python offers no hook to reject those statically without also breaking __repr__ and __eq__, which are exactly what make the marker debuggable, so this is a documented limitation rather than a defect to fix in the type system. Two consequences to schedule. First, a lint or grep check over unions carrying a fail-closed member looking for the three escaping shapes, since that is the only place they can be caught before runtime; generation/persistence.py prescribed bool(declared_personalizable_slots) in prose as the house recipe until #760 corrected it, so the idiom has already propagated once and nothing would have caught the second copy. Second, an audit of this repo's other tri-states for the same falsy-collision shape: AL-306 (check_device_vocabulary.py), AL-582 (FallbackProvider.resolved_provider) and AL-583 (the reviewer-independence check) are three prior occurrences, and nothing yet detects a fourth. Also recorded here rather than fixed: moderation/rescreen.py's per-book except Exception converts the new runtime TypeError into one outcome="error" WARNING for that book, so on exactly one of the four containment paths the marker's promise of "raise loudly rather than answer wrongly" is muffled into a completed-looking sweep. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C378 AL-625: Gate D3's advisory-persistence floor recalibration has no floor value that satisfies both stated constraints, and needs an owner ruling on a design change rather than a number. Measuring candidate floors {0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10} against the 2026-08-01 Stage-0 baseline found the clean-noise target (<= 0.2 advisories/node) and the zero-adversarial-loss target move in opposite directions across the whole range; only 0.10 clears the clean target and it loses 10 of 14 adversarial pairs. The measurement's own recommendation is per-category floors (a low floor on self-harm*/sexual*/harassment/threatening/illicit, a higher one on violence/violence/graphic) rather than a single scalar. Ratified 2026-08-25: the project owner adopted per-category floors, keeping a low floor on self-harm*/sexual*/harassment/threatening/illicit and a higher floor (indicatively 0.10) on violence/violence/graphic; exact values are to be set when implemented, folding in the planned adversarial-corpus v1.1 growth. _ADVISORY_SCORE_FLOOR stays at 0.01 until that lands. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C380 AL-627: _PERSPECTIVE_BRIGHTLINE_MIN_SCORE = 0.8 (scripts/capture_stage0_baseline.py) lost its only pinning test in the same commit that retired Perspective as a Stage-0 signal source. a5abb2b4 deleted _run_perspective/_perspective_attribute_finding and unwired the pipeline call site; the test file's test_recorded_perspective_brightline_matches_the_live_classifier, which asserted the recorded constant matched the live classifier's own block threshold, imported _perspective_attribute_finding directly and had to be deleted alongside it to clear an ImportError, rather than rewritten against a replacement.     UW-C381 AL-628: The frontend's approval-override gate re-derives the backend's severe-finding predicate in TypeScript instead of consuming a server-computed field, and the two can drift without either side's tests noticing. frontend/src/admin/ReviewDetailPage.tsx's needsOverride computes, over allFindings, whether any finding has verdict block or has verdict flag with severity high, guarded only by a #CRITICAL: security comment warning it must stay in lockstep with the backend rather than by a shared contract or a cross-repo test. The backend's own equivalent, severe_finding_counts() (src/cyo_adventure/moderation/report.py), is a separate implementation over the same Finding shape. A future change to either side's severity rule (for example scoping severe_finding_counts() to exclude structural findings, see UW-C382) has no mechanism forcing the other to follow, so the override-reason prompt could show for a book the backend would not actually gate, or vice versa. Proposed: have the backend compute and serve a boolean (or the same counts severe_finding_counts() already produces) on the review-detail response, and have the frontend consume it rather than re-deriving the predicate; this is a contract change, so it needs the OpenAPI regeneration workflow, not a quiet edit. 4b unscheduled
    UW-C382 AL-629: severe_finding_counts() counts a collapsed pipeline artifact toward the approval override gate, while its sibling tiered counters in the same codebase deliberately exclude structural findings. moderation/stages.py collapses a per-node moderation-reviewer outage into one story-level Finding(verdict=Verdict.FLAG, severity=FindingSeverity.HIGH, structural=True, concern="reviewer_unavailable") rather than flooding the queue with one finding per node (the fix AL-624/UW-C377 describes for the write-once-report problem this same PR closes). severe_finding_counts(), which feeds the admin override-reason requirement, counts BLOCK/high-FLAG verdicts with no check of the structural field, so this collapsed artifact counts as a "high" and could require a written override reason for a book whose only severe signal is that moderation itself was unavailable, not a content judgment. Its sibling counters explicitly guard against exactly this: api/review_surface.py:724 (f.verdict == Verdict.FLAG and not f.structural) and moderation/repair.py:90 both exclude structural findings from their tiered counts. Untested either way today: no test exercises severe_finding_counts() against a structural HIGH-FLAG finding to pin the current (inclusive) behavior or to catch a change. Proposed: an owner ruling on whether the override gate should exclude structural findings to match its siblings, or whether requiring an override reason for "moderation was unavailable" is intentionally conservative; either way, add the missing test once the ruling lands. 4b unscheduled
    UW-C383 AL-630: moderation_report_unusable() clears a report the moment ONE finding carries a genuine verdict, so a book can pass the approval gate with most of its nodes never actually judged, and the admin review surface renders those unjudged nodes as clean rather than as unreviewed. Two independent mechanisms compound. First, the predicate itself (moderation/report.py) is existence-of-one-genuine-finding, not coverage-of-nodes: the loop over findings returns False (usable) at the first entry whose verdict is a non-empty string, with no comparison of judged-node count against total nodes. Second, moderation/stages.py's soft stages (lines ~746, ~796) call _parse_verdict(..., fail_safe=Verdict.PASS), so a soft-stage fail-safe (the classifier could not be reached or its response could not be parsed for that node) is stored as an ordinary Verdict.PASS, indistinguishable in the persisted report from a node the reviewer actually read and passed; and api/review_surface.py:165 (if view.verdict is Verdict.PASS: continue) filters PASS-verdict rows out of what the human reviewer sees, so those nodes are not merely unlabelled, they are invisible on the review screen. Reported (not independently re-measured here; attribute the counts to whoever ran the census): sk_clocktower_cipher with 22 of 25 nodes carrying no readability verdict, sk_hollow_lighthouse with 24 of 148, and one node each on two further books. Net effect: a book can clear the mandatory-human-approval gate this PR hardens while most of its content was never judged by anything, and the reviewer has no visual cue that coverage was incomplete. Proposed: make moderation_report_unusable() (or a sibling coverage check) compare judged-node count against total node count rather than testing for the existence of one genuine finding, and/or stop collapsing a soft-stage fail-safe into Verdict.PASS so it stays distinguishable from a real pass on the review surface. This is a design change beyond this PR's scope and needs an owner ruling. 4b unscheduled
    UW-C384 AL-631: Two of moderation_report_unusable()'s three detection arms are structurally dead against the 12-book legacy corpus the predicate exists to find, leaving one string-literal check as the sole detector for that population. The provider-level independence flag is hardcoded True for the mock reviewer (moderation/review_provider.py:119, return MockProvider(...), True); the arm that actually writes reviewer_independent = False plus the mock_reviewer_active structural finding is moderation/pipeline.py::_stamp_mock_reviewer, gated on review_provider == "mock" and environment != "local" and introduced in 8ca8d1b3 (2026-07-30, PR #496). The mock-reviewer incident that produced the 12 in-review production books referenced throughout this PR (AL-624) predates that stamp by nine days (2026-07-21), and moderation_report is write-once JSONB, so none of those 12 rows carry either the stamp or the concern; both the predicate's reviewer_independent is False arm and scripts/remoderate_books.py --mock-moderated's corresponding stamp check cannot match them. Only the FAIL_SAFE_MESSAGE_SUBSTRING message-literal arm reaches this exact population, making a single string literal the sole detector for the population this work exists to find; AL-624 already states the underlying principle (the signal must come from the report's own content, not from field presence or recency), so this row is the residual single-point-of-failure that principle leaves in place rather than a new discovery. Proposed: treat the message-literal arm as safety-critical and pin it with a regression test (tracked separately as part of this PR's test coverage), and record here that any future change to the fail-safe message text or constant silently un-detects the legacy books with no other arm to catch the regression. 4b unscheduled
    UW-C385 AL-632: check-rad-citations's pre-commit hook cannot see a test rename or deletion, by its own documented design, and the blast radius stays invisible until an unrelated later commit happens to touch the citing file. Verified against the script's own module docstring: the hook's files: filter covers only src/, scripts/, frontend/src/, and the baseline file, explicitly excluding tests/; the docstring itself already states "a commit that only deletes or renames a test file, touching nothing the hook is scoped to, is invisible to the hook and still caught by CI." --all mode (already run by the rad-citations CI job on every push, pull_request, and merge_group) already performs the tree-wide resolution this row might otherwise propose as new; the gap is purely local timing, not missing capability. Proposed: detect a staged rename or deletion of a tests/**/test_*.py file at pre-commit time (git diff --cached --diff-filter=RD) and escalate only that case to a full --all resolution, so the developer's own commit catches the orphaned citation instead of waiting for CI or an incidental later commit to the citing file. Scope note: this row covers only the shape the hook CANNOT catch (a rename orphaning a citation in a DIFFERENT file than the one being renamed). Two sibling shapes from the same branch and session, where the hook caught the problem as designed, are recorded separately in UW-C386/AL-633 rather than folded in here, since the fix each shape argues for is different. 4b unscheduled

    UW-C390 AL-637: The hidden-fail-safe scan is retro-only by construction and cannot see a report written after 2026-08-14.     UW-C394 AL-643: A book's systematic apostrophe-transcription fault was absorbed into a new rule's measured noise floor, and it is not the only book with the fault. out/the-sunken-temple.filled.json contains zero apostrophe characters of any kind ("Heddas seal") while sibling artifacts carry hundreds, so its possessives parse as plurals; that single missing punctuation mark inflates the book to 33 of PN-1's 135 corpus findings, eleven of which are duplicate rows from a name reported twice, once bare and once suffixed (Hedda and Heddas, and ten more like it), so 22 of the 33 findings cover just 11 entities. out/the-teddy-bears-picnic.filled.json (29 nodes) is also apostrophe-free across every node body, contributing 1 finding and no doubling, so the defect spans at least two artifacts even though its magnitude is concentrated in one. The findings were deliberately left un-unified, because inferring an absent apostrophe would merge genuine plurals into possessives and hide real cases. Proposed: an artifact-level fill sanity check that reports a filled book with zero apostrophes across dozens of nodes as ONE fill defect, so a transcription fault is attributed to the artifact rather than spread across whichever rule reads that text next; then re-derive PN-1's noise floor from a corpus without the affected artifacts, since the published 2.97-per-100 figure is an upper bound.     UW-C395 AL-644: A prose defect in a published book has no in-place remedy, and the only exit from published removes the book from the reader. api/node_edit.py permits an edit only while a storybook is in_review or needs_revision, never published or archived; publishing/state_machine.py's LEGAL_TRANSITIONS offers exactly one transition out of PUBLISHED (ARCHIVE to ARCHIVED) and ARCHIVED is absorbing. Concrete instance: sk_cave_of_echoes v1, 64 nodes, one reader mid-book and two assignments, needs a three-word gloss on Biscuit (named in 64 of 64 nodes, "dog" in none) and a consistency pass over 23 nodes that write Maya as a literal while 41 nodes carry a {~HERO:Maya~} sentinel, so a personalized reader currently meets both her own name and Maya's in the same book. The immutability guarantee is correct and must not be weakened: a book a child is reading must not change under her. Owner decision this row carries: how a published book's prose defect is remediated at all. Candidate shapes are a REVISE transition from PUBLISHED to NEEDS_REVISION that keeps the published version readable until a successor is approved, or a documented supersede-and-reassign procedure that never leaves a reader with nothing. Explicitly out of scope: widening node_edit's status guard to mutate a published blob in place. 4b decision

    UW-C405 AL-662: PL-25 is structural but edit_node is prose-only, so the word-equivalence relaxation is the only reachable fix for a book in review, and it forces the opening node past its own advisory ceiling. sk_sunken_temple's n_start is itself the first decision (depth 1 against a floor of 2); inserting a choiceless establishing node is the fix the rule wants and edit_node forbids it.     UW-C406 AL-663: scripts/remoderate_books.py collapses failed, blocked, excluded, timed_out and not_attempted into one sys.exit(<str>), always exit 1, so a retry loop cannot distinguish a permanent hard block from a retryable rollback. sweep.sh retried sk_vanishing_orchard three times in 15 seconds against a verdict that cannot change without a prose edit, re-spending an LLM review pass each time. The exit code was designed as a human attention signal and reused as a machine retry signal. Give blocked and excluded a distinct exit code, break the sweep loop on it, and add a --json summary. Same shape recurs in moderation/classifiers.py::_run_openai, which catches base httpx.HTTPError so a permanent 401 retries like a transient 429; fix with the _TRANSIENT_STATUS split the three generation providers already use. DONE 2026-08-27: both halves landed on fix/published-slot-contract-false-block. db03e57d splits the exit codes (1 = retryable: failed/timed_out/not_attempted; 3 = needs a human: blocked/excluded; 3 rather than 2 because argparse owns 2 for usage errors) and breaks the sweep loop on 3. 0a2a05b3 fixes the classifiers.py recurrence by moving the retry decision onto the exception (ClassifierUnavailable(retryable=, disables_classifier=)) with an httpx.HTTPStatusError arm ordered ahead of the (httpx.HTTPError, ValueError) arm it subclasses; a credential rejection now latches the circuit on the first node instead of re-proving itself on every remaining one. Both keyword defaults are the safe answer, so raise sites that predate the change keep retrying.     UW-C407 AL-664: one failed review batch removes 8 nodes from safety coverage and downgrades the book's hard block to a soft flag, so an incompletely-reviewed book reports as a successful pass. stages.py:752 raises the shortfall as a structural=True/HIGH/reviewer_unavailable finding whose verdict is Verdict.FLAG; report.py::has_hard_block is any(verdict is BLOCK), so the flag clears the hard block, has_soft_flag becomes true, remoderate_books exits 0 and the sweep prints soft-flagged, review when convenient.     UW-C409 AL-666: editing a shell script while an instance of it is running corrupts that run and misreports the faulting line.     UW-C433 Six workflows still upload a Playwright output directory wholesale, and a Playwright output directory is credential-bearing regardless of its trace configuration. accessibility-compliance-weekly.yml, cross-device-e2e.yml, e2e-real-nightly.yml, e2e-real-pr-smoke.yml, usersim.yml, and webkit-kid.yml each publish frontend/test-results/ in full.     UW-C436 The RLS-blindness guard on the engagement job closes the dynamic-import evasion but not a getattr-constructed one. tests/unit/test_engagement_correlation_job.py walks ast.Name, ast.Attribute, and ast.ImportFrom, which kills the __import__('...').get_session() mutant that SURVIVED the guard's first version (see AL-708).     UW-C437 NARROW_UPLOAD_ALLOWLIST has no stated criterion for what makes a file clear to upload, so the allowlist grows by whoever is adding a file rather than against a rule. frontend/scripts/check-artifact-upload-safety.mjs classifies an upload path as narrow, wholesale, or unrelated, and a narrow path is permitted; after 926cb474 the set holds exactly one name, leaked-device-grants.jsonl. The guard exists because Playwright's error-context.md renders an accessibility snapshot that includes a password field's value, and this repository is public, so every uploaded artifact is world-readable for its whole retention window. Nothing is leaking today and this row is not a live finding, but re-derive every number below before citing it again rather than carrying it forward, since that is exactly how this row went wrong once already: the e2e fixtures do carry synthetic profile-shaped fields, for example display_name, which appears 46 times under frontend/e2e (grep -rno "display_name" frontend/e2e \| wc -l, run 2026-08-29; the count is scope-sensitive, since adding frontend/e2e-usersim brings it to 47 and the full set of seven frontend/e2e* directories brings it to 64), seeded with values such as 'Remy', 'Reader A', 'Kid A', and 'Ada' (grep -rhoP "display_name: '.*?'" frontend/e2e \| sort -u), never a real child's name. An age-like field is also present but is not given an occurrence count here: age, age_band, and ageBand each match a different, non-overlapping set of lines depending on scope and pattern (candidates ranged from 4 to 112 across the variants tried), so no single number was reproducible and none is asserted. The claim that both real-environment tiers already narrow their uploads is false: e2e-real-nightly.yml and e2e-real-pr-smoke.yml both upload frontend/test-results/ wholesale, confirmed by calling the guard's own findWholesaleUploads('.github/workflows') (2026-08-29), which lists both by name; e2e-staging.yml is the only tier that narrows, to frontend/test-results/leaked-device-grants.jsonl (grep -n 'upload-artifact\|path:' .github/workflows/e2e-staging.yml); and e2e-prod.yml carries no upload-artifact step at all (grep -c upload-artifact .github/workflows/e2e-prod.yml returns 0). The gap is that none of that is written down as a condition. A future editor adding a second entry has no question to answer beyond "does my file help triage", and the reviewer has nothing to check the answer against; the same edit is safe when the fixture is synthetic and a children's-privacy incident when it is seeded from a real profile, and the diff looks identical either way. Proposed: state the admission criterion beside the constant (what a candidate file may contain, who decides, and what evidence the PR must show), and consider whether the guard should assert the criterion mechanically rather than trust it, for example by requiring an allowlisted file to be produced by a named writer in the repo rather than by whatever the run happens to drop at that path. Raised by the outside model panel on PR #780 (glm-5.2) and kept as a design row rather than patched, because picking the criterion is an owner decision, not a code change; the row's counts and example strings were rewritten 2026-08-29 after an independent audit found the original numbers unreproducible and the original examples absent from the tree. 5 unscheduled
    UW-C438 AL-716: markdownlint is configured at pre-push in a repository with no installed pre-push hook and no CI job that runs it, so it enforces nothing. .pre-commit-config.yaml:543 places the markdownlint-cli hook under stages: [pre-push]; .git/hooks/ contains only pre-commit; grep -rn markdownlint .github/workflows/ returns nothing (all three re-checked 2026-08-30). The 13 agent reports landed in docs/planning/evidence/brief-review-2026-08-22/ were deliberately left byte-identical to the source branch rather than reformatted, because their README states they are preserved as delivered and because reformatting them would not have made the hook pass.     UW-C464 AL-739: a recorded cost computed with exact Decimal arithmetic is invertible, so a rate card can be falsified from the artifact alone. Five legs each recovered their input-token count as an exact integer under exactly one card and under no other, which is what identified UW-C463. Add cost-card inversion to the vendor-comparison script as a self-check so an artifact reports its own billing inconsistency. CI hygiene unscheduled
    UW-C465 AL-740: a caveat written as a YAML comment is discarded by every parser. if-corpus-licensing.yaml held "this is not a legal clearance" in its # header; yaml.safe_load returned five keys and none recorded it, leaving the file one naive comprehension away from being read as a clearance list. Fixed in place with is_legal_clearance: false; sweep the remaining datasets under docs/planning/data/ for load-bearing caveats that live only in comments. doc unscheduled
    UW-C466 AL-741: a field named for a permission held a claim read off a third-party banner. commercial_use_permitted: true rested on a Wikisource public-domain notice, which grants nothing and addresses neither commercial use nor derivatives. Renamed to commercial_use_claimed / derivatives_claimed and nulled. Sweep the planning datasets for *_permitted, *_allowed and *_approved fields whose values are derived rather than granted. doc unscheduled
    UW-C467 AL-742: an absent verification_method is indistinguishable from an unverified row. Twenty of 35 source rows in craft-benchmark-corpus.yaml omitted it while having in fact been checked by the weakest defined method, so omission both faked a coverage gap and hid the weakness of the covered rows. All 35 now carry an explicit value; a checker over docs/planning/data/ erroring on a missing method field would make it mechanical. doc unscheduled
    UW-C468 AL-743: yamllint --strict promotes a level: warning rule to a failing exit, so a file clean under the bare tool fails the hook. 53 line-length findings, all configured as warnings, passed yamllint and failed pre-commit. Same shape as the isolated-ruff trap: check a file with pre-commit run <hook-id> --files <path>, never the bare tool. CI hygiene unscheduled
    UW-C469 AL-744: markdownlint is declared stages: [pre-push], the pre-push git hook is not installed, and CI runs only interrogate tree-wide, so MD060 is enforced by nothing. Verified on 2026-08-30: .git/hooks/ holds pre-commit but only pre-push.sample, and ci.yml:737 is the sole tree-wide hook invocation. The register file already carries pre-existing MD060 debt on main, which is what a declared-but-unrun rule produces. Decide the rule's status: move it to the commit stage and clear the debt, run the pre-push stage in CI, or delete the hook. CI hygiene unscheduled
    UW-C470 AL-745: a banner asserting a closed count of superseded findings understated by seven. The brief-review evidence set claimed two findings overtaken; sweeping all fourteen found nine moved (R-1, R-6, R-7 fully; R-5, R-11, R-12 partly; R-2, R-3, R-9 noted but uncounted), and R-7's own replacement figure was itself wrong against the census. State supersession non-exhaustively and carry status per finding, not in a top-of-file aggregate. doc unscheduled
    UW-C471 AL-746: a concurrent agent's pre-commit stash and pop silently destroyed a peer's unstaged hunks in two files during PR #792. git fsck --lost-found recovered nothing because unstaged content is never objectified. Give concurrent agents separate worktrees when they may touch the same paths; where a shared tree is unavoidable, stage and commit at every checkpoint before any peer commits. CI hygiene unscheduled
    UW-C472 AL-747: three independent evidence documents concluded no hard-block guard exists from a symbol grep that is an anti-oracle. grep has_hard_block src/cyo_adventure/publishing/ returns zero while four guards enforce the block through severe_finding_counts(); the claim survived two re-verifications because each repeated the same probe, and nine further passages inherited it. Require an end-to-end function read for any "nothing guards X" finding, and re-derivation by a different method on re-verification. doc unscheduled
    UW-C473 AL-748: the #CRITICAL comment at src/cyo_adventure/api/node_edit.py:793 asserts a false invariant and its #VERIFY test cannot catch it. It states approve() "only requires SOME moderation_report to exist", which publishing/service.py:474 falsified on 2026-08-25; the cited test pins the local write-persistence mechanism and passes regardless. Correct the comment to name the four guards, and adopt the rule that a RAD marker asserts invariants only about the code it annotates. Two evidence documents cited this comment as authority. now unscheduled
    UW-C474 GA-D1's five accountability requirements own no W item and no ledger row. The 2026-08-22 remediation plan routes GA-D1 hard-block ruling to "GA-D1's own accountability requirements", which nothing schedules. The ruling is now largely closed by the ADR-005 amendments of 2026-08-25 and 2026-08-28, leaving three residuals that still need a home: override records carry finding counts rather than durable finding ids over a mutable moderation_report JSONB column; approval binds to a version row rather than to a digest of the artifact reviewed (node_edit.py:809 reassigns blob on that row); and there is no separation of duties, so a dual-role adult can approve their own family's blocked book with a recorded reason. Owner decision needed on which, if any, become work. now unscheduled
    UW-C475 AL-749 open: the admin review surface's volume problem is 97.0% self-inflicted, and the earlier diagnosis (AL-036, UW-C02) proposed pagination for it. api/review_surface.py:334-360 fans each merged finding across every entry in node_ids, emitting one full-prose passage card per affected node; on the largest queued book that turns 1 blocking finding plus 14 flags into 610 finding instances across 391 passage articles, 580 of those instances OpenAI provider boilerplate advisories, for a 2.5% signal-to-noise ratio (15 of 610) and 102 screens of scrolling before the ranked list that states the block in one line. The two figures are different denominators and the earlier wording called the 610 "cards", which read as if 610 articles rendered: the plan's severity census (section 2.1) counts 595 Advisory plus 14 Flagged plus 1 Blocked badge inside 391 articles. The split that fixes it already exists and is bypassed: _rank_and_split (:519-545) routes LOW+ADVISORY findings into low_advisory_findings, which ReviewDetailPage.tsx:728-741 already renders collapsed, but the fan-out runs earlier and never consults it. Scoped as RS-A1 in docs/planning/review-screen-remediation-plan-2026-08-31.md, which is the plan this row schedules from. Note the general lesson, which is the reason this is a register row and not just a bug: AL-036 was a competent review that measured the DOM and proposed a rendering fix, and pagination would have made 250 screens of boilerplate navigable rather than removing it, while reading as a fix and closing the row. Before optimising how a large collection renders, measure how much of it the server should not have sent. now unscheduled
    UW-C476 AL-751 open: the calibration premise behind RS-CAL3/RS-CAL4 is inverted, and correcting it changes what paid spend would buy. Replaying all 31 stored production moderation reports (docs/planning/safety/production-floor-replay-2026-08-31.md) shows the ratified per-category floor removes 0.2% of surfaced occurrences on band 13-16 and 0.6% on 16+, while the already-shipped RS-A1 low-advisory collapse removes 96-100% at no recall cost: all 14 findings spanning 40 or more nodes are low-severity openai advisories on violence/violence/graphic scoring 0.33-0.45. So the reviewer-load question RS-CAL3 was scoped to answer is already answered, by a change that shipped. The one floor with real leverage is violence* near 0.50 (71-90% of remaining occurrences), and it sits INSIDE the graded range, so it trades recall rather than trimming noise. Re-scope RS-CAL3 to the two narrow questions that remain (does a 0.50 violence* floor drop a true positive; is anything at all measurable below 0.01) before authorising a fresh six-band capture, and re-scope RS-CAL4 to rule on recall rather than on load. RS-B4 stays blocked behind both. The general lesson, and the reason this is a register row: calibrate against the stored production population before paying for a fresh one, because a fixture corpus is a shape check and was wrong here by two orders of magnitude. 5 unscheduled
    UW-C477 OutstandingDecisionItem is a flat model that can express four states where only two occur; make it a discriminated union. src/cyo_adventure/api/schemas.py::OutstandingDecisionItem (RS-C2/RS-C3) carries kind: Literal["moderation", "cover"] alongside two nullable detail fields, moderation and cover, so the type admits both-set and neither-set rows that the surface never produces. The invariant is real and enforced at the producer, not merely hoped for: api/approval.py::get_outstanding_decisions appends a moderation row only inside if moderation is not None, and a cover row only while constructing its OutstandingCoverDetail. The proper fix is a discriminated union of two models, one per kind, so the type says what the producer already guarantees. Deferred deliberately, and this row exists because the model's own docstring cites this ID: the union rewrites the wire schema into a oneOf, which regenerates the shape of the committed client (frontend/src/client/types.gen.ts) and re-narrows every consumer in frontend/src/admin/OutstandingDecisionsPage.tsx. That is a refactor of a shipped contract, not a defect fix, and it was out of scope for PR #797, whose purpose was clearing a merge block. Nothing is wrong today; what is missing is the type-level guarantee, so a future producer or a hand-built row can violate the invariant without the schema objecting. 5 unscheduled
    UW-C478 AL-762: the frontend coverage-matrix drift-guard is CI-only, so a clean local pre-commit run is not evidence a new test file is indexed. scripts/check_coverage_matrix.py is invoked once, at .github/workflows/ci.yml:217, and appears nowhere in .pre-commit-config.yaml. It caught a real omission on PR #797 (frontend/src/guardian/findingCounts.test.ts, one of five added Vitest files, unindexed) and named both the file and the remedy, so the guard's logic is sound; what is missing is a local host for it. The cost is a full red CI round-trip per omission, paid by whoever adds a test file, and the failure arrives after a push rather than at the commit that caused it. Proposed: add it as a repo-local pre-commit hook with always_run: true and pass_filenames: false, because the script enumerates the tree rather than reading staged paths, so the usual staged-files hook shape would not fire on a matrix edit that orphans an existing entry. 5 unscheduled

    Cluster D: untracked GitHub issues¶

    roadmap.md lines 197-199 assert that open issues "remain accurately tracked" in the debt register. That claim is false: the debt register cites 9 issue numbers, 6 still open, against 48 open issues as of 2026-08-03. The 34 issues below appear in no other planning document by number or by description, so this cluster is their only phase home.

    Adding a row here is now a judgement call rather than a gate. scripts/check_work_linkage.py once carried a --check-issue-orphans flag requiring every open GitHub issue to be cited under docs/planning/ or labelled unplanned, plus a --check-issues flag resolving each cited number against the API. Both were retired: they ran only in CI, because they needed network access and gh auth that the pre-commit hook deliberately does not have, so the two gates enforced different contracts and a green local run proved nothing about the stricter one. The recurring cost also outgrew the return, most visibly when the workflow's own drift-alert issue became an uncited open issue and failed the gate that filed it. What remains is the offline linkage contract, which every row in this table is still bound by.

    UW-D26 (no issue) Standing problem, not a defect in any one run, and the reason the three rows above exist at all. Scheduled workflows file ci-failure issues that are deduplicated by title but never auto-closed, and an open uncited issue is an orphan, so every bot alert fails the very gate that files it. #575 is the pure case: the linkage check filed an issue that then made the linkage check fail. The considered fixes were to label bot issues unplanned at filing time, to exempt ci-failure in --check-issue-orphans, or to close the issue from the workflow on a green run. Decided instead to remove both issue-facing checks (--check-issues and --check-issue-orphans): tying every open issue to the phase plan was a good idea that cost more than it returned, and an offline checker is also one that the pre-commit hook and CI can run identically. Removal landed in PR #576; the checker now makes no network call and the intro to this cluster records why. CI hygiene done

    UW-D28 #557 Alert on security events rather than only emitting them. Neither wires a notification channel, so security-events.md section 6 still describes zero shipped alert rules and section 2's "Alert on" lines remain a specification. Three specifics the issue body predates: the rules live in homelab-infra, not this repo; security_event_write_failed needs its own alert-on-any-occurrence rule, because fail-open means a silent audit gap looks exactly like an absence of attacks; and the security_event table has no retention or purge mechanism at all, which is an ADR-018 gap the append-only trigger makes harder to close, not easier. Note the issue body cites UW-D19 as its register home, which is wrong: that ID is #505, the unfixed kernel-header CVEs. This row is the correct one. 5 unscheduled
    UW-D29 #601 Original finding, superseded below by PR #604; kept as the record of why this row exists. supabase/config.toml was committed but never deployed: supabase-staging.yml/supabase-production.yml run supabase db push (migrations) only, no config push step exists anywhere in .github/workflows/. Production's real [auth] policy (password requirements, rate limits, captcha) is whatever was last set by hand in the dashboard, unreviewable and undocumented, including the only throttle on the unauthenticated resetPasswordForEmail path. Previously deferred as design-spec follow-up 3 in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-10-supabase-environments-pipeline-design.md pending CLI stability; supabase config push is confirmed supported at the pinned CLI version 2.109.1, so that reason no longer holds. The prerequisite blocker (production was adopted from an already-live project and its [auth] block had never been reconciled against config.toml) is cleared: both projects' live auth config were read from the Management API and diffed field by field against the file on 2026-08-04, and the diff was reviewed before anything was written. Every divergence ran in the loosening direction, confirming the checked-in file was a local-dev file rather than an aspirational policy. Delivered in PR #604: config.toml now carries the agreed policy (password floor 10 per NIST SP 800-63B, email confirmations on, OTP length 8, TOTP MFA on, a 60s email throttle via [auth.email].max_frequency, reauthentication required for password change). Note that [auth.rate_limit].email_sent is a different setting and is not live: it needs custom SMTP, tracked separately as UW-D30 plus [remotes.staging]/[remotes.production] blocks for the two genuinely per-environment fields, and config push runs in both deploy workflows between link and db push, with a log assertion that the expected [remotes.*] override actually loaded rather than leaving that check to a human reading the run log. Staging is deployed and verified against a pre-push snapshot with a delta of exactly the two intended fields. Production is wired but has never been dispatched, which is why this row stays at verify rather than closing: it sits behind the production GitHub Environment's required-reviewer gate, so the first production rollout is a human action rather than a merge side effect, and nothing else in the plan tracks that action. A row marked done here would be the only record, and it would say the work is finished when half of it has not run. Closed 2026-08-04: every step of that checklist ran. Staging deployed automatically on the merge of #604 (run 30933782746); production deployed by human-approved dispatch (run 30934654664) after a pre-push snapshot of all five surfaces. Both runs printed Loading config override: [remotes.<env>] and passed the fail-closed ref guard, and both reported API, DB and Storage already up to date, so the delta stayed confined to [auth] as designed. Production moved exactly four fields, established by diffing the Management API field by field against the snapshot rather than by reading the run log: password_min_length 6 to 10, security_update_password_require_reauthentication false to true, password_required_characters null to "" (equivalent), and uri_allow_list gaining https://cyo.williamshome.family/**. Nothing else on the auth surface moved and the other four surfaces were byte-identical. The three deliberate omissions held: Google OAuth still enabled with its client_id intact, captcha and HIBP left unmanaged rather than asserted off. Two things a future reader would otherwise rediscover. First, the staging run was not the zero-delta predicted: it applied secure_password_change false to true, a field tightened during review of #604 after staging had been reconciled by hand, which is git asserting itself over a manual setting rather than drift. Second, the reset-flow #VERIFY that gated this row's own risk has now been exercised against production and the feared deadlock is not real; see supabase/config.toml's marker for the evidence. Promotion procedure and the snapshot/restore recovery path: docs/guides/supabase-environments.md section 4. Follow-on: UW-D30. 5 done
    UW-D30 (no issue) No custom SMTP provider is configured on either Supabase project, so [auth.rate_limit].email_sent in supabase/config.toml is not deployed. Split out of UW-D29, which found it: the CLI sends rate_limit_email_sent only when [auth.email.smtp] is enabled, and smtp_host reads null on both project refs, so auth email goes through Supabase's built-in service under that service's own project-wide cap. The practical effect is that the value in the file is inert, and the only throttles actually protecting the unauthenticated resetPasswordForEmail path (frontend/src/auth/AuthContext.tsx) are smtp_max_frequency, set to 60s, plus the built-in cap, which is not ours to tune. A config value that looks like policy but is inert is worse than an absent one, so the file carries a #CRITICAL marker saying so until this is fixed. Also blocks any transactional email beyond the built-in service's deliverability and volume limits. To do: provision an SMTP provider, add [auth.email.smtp], then confirm the configured email_sent starts appearing in the Management API's rate_limit_email_sent. Related: leaked-password protection (password_hibp_enabled) is separately unavailable, returning HTTP 402 on the free plan and having no config.toml key at CLI 2.109.1; both are recorded in the file's [auth] comments. 5 unscheduled

    UW-E17 GA-D1: a moderation hard block is not a publish-blocking condition, so a hard-blocked book can be published in two clicks and the override leaves no record. Editing any node of an in_review book merges a fresh BLOCK while the status stays in_review, and publishing/service.py:412 guards only moderation_report is None; has_hard_block is read at eight sites, all inside moderation/, and no database constraint exists across the 67 migrations on main as of 2026-08-30. The behaviour is deliberate: api/node_edit.py carries it as a #CRITICAL ADR-005 position ("surfaced, never rejects the write"), tests/unit/test_node_edit.py:846 locks it, and the service comment states the invariant as no unmoderated path reaching published, which is screened-ness rather than block-freeness. So this is a ruling to make, not a patch to write. Step 0 before anything else: query production for published versions carrying summary.hard_block: true; a nonzero count makes this a live incident rather than a latent gap. That query needs an authorised supabase MCP connection and could not be run from the analysing session. Owner to rule: the project owner, between (a) hard block becomes a publish-blocking condition, and (b) the override stays and becomes accountable. The review recommends (b), because (a) contradicts a twice-affirmed ADR and removes a legitimate admin escape hatch. Under either ruling the same five accountability requirements stand, and they are the real defects: a typed override reason required on ApproveBody when has_hard_block is true; the blocking finding ids copied onto the approval record at approve time, so the override survives a later re-moderation; a version hash pinned in ApproveBody so approval binds to the exact artifact reviewed; separation of duties, so an adult holding both guardian and admin cannot approve a hard-blocked book for their own family; and an override event in events/ plus a distinct admin-dashboard surface, so overrides are countable. Distinct from UW-L08, which tracks two already-published books holding severe findings rather than the override path itself. Source: docs/planning/cyo-brief-gap-remediation-plan-2026-08-22.md, GA-D1. 5 decision

    UW-F24 setup_logging() is never called by the served application, so three settings that exist, are documented, and have tests are inert in production. Found 2026-07-31 by the issue #516 generalisation sweep (an AST scan for every attribute read of settings across src/ and scripts/, after a naive name grep produced a false all-clear). Verified: structlog.is_configured() returns False after create_app(), and the only caller anywhere is scripts/backfill_covers_r2.py, a one-off backfill that passes hardcoded arguments rather than settings. Neither RQ worker entrypoint calls it either. Consequences: Settings.log_level, json_logs, and include_timestamp change nothing at runtime; the deployed service renders through structlog's default ConsoleRenderer rather than the JSON handler json_logs=True selects; and add_correlation_id is never installed as a processor, so log lines carry no correlation_id key even though CorrelationMiddleware populates the contextvar and CLAUDE.md's "Example JSON Log Output" shows one. Explicit get_correlation_id() reads still work; only the automatic injection into every log line is missing. Fix is to call setup_logging() from create_app() and both worker entrypoints with the settings values, which will change the log format that container stdout consumers see, so it is a deployment-visible change and not a pure code fix. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F25 Settings.forwarded_allow_ips is never read in Python. Found by the same 2026-07-31 sweep. The value that actually takes effect is uvicorn's --forwarded-allow-ips CLI flag: hardcoded to 172.16.0.0/12 in the Dockerfile CMD and overridden per stack in docker-compose.yml (${FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS:-172.26.0.0/16}) and docker-compose.prod.yml. Setting CYO_ADVENTURE_FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS in an environment therefore does nothing, while the field's docstring reads as though it governs the behaviour. Security-adjacent: this is the list of proxies trusted to set X-Forwarded-*, so the resolution is either to have the entrypoint derive the flag from the setting (one source of truth) or to delete the field and repoint its rationale at the Dockerfile and compose files. Do not simply drop it without recording where the real value lives; the docstring is currently the only prose explanation of the choice. Note that vulture no longer reports this: the UW-F23 baseline suppresses it (tagged UW-F25 inline in vulture_whitelist.py), so this row is now the only signal keeping it visible. Delete that whitelist entry as part of the fix. 5 unscheduled

    UW-F31 The no-em-dash pre-commit hook (.pre-commit-config.yaml:466-470) has never fired and cannot fire as written. Its entry is git grep --cached -nP "\xe2\x80\x94" -- .; git grep -P runs PCRE in UTF-8 mode against UTF-8 content, where \xe2 denotes codepoint U+00E2 (a-circumflex), not byte 0xE2, so the pattern searches for a three-codepoint mojibake sequence that never occurs in a correctly-encoded file. Mechanism confirmed 2026-08-11 by reproduction against this repository's index (git version 2.43.0, LANG=C.UTF-8), three runs of the same command differing only in the pattern: the hook's own pattern returns 0 matches; \x{2014} returns 59; a pattern of literal em-dash bytes ("$(printf '\xe2\x80\x94')") also returns 59. That third result explains the earlier disconfirming test recorded on this row: an interactive shell that sends the raw bytes (a pasted em-dash, $'...', echo -e) is not testing the pattern the YAML passes, which arrives at PCRE as the eight literal characters \xe2\x80\x94. pass_filenames: false is a real but secondary property: the hook scans the whole tracked tree and still matches nothing. Corrected count: 59 em-dashes across 16 tracked files, not 40+. Found in review of PR #683 (2026-08-11), which shipped a literal U+2014 into an ADR through a green pre-commit run and fully green CI. The correct pattern is \x{2014}. Fix the regex and clear the backlog in the same change: repairing the pattern alone converts a silently-inert hook into one that blocks every commit in the repo. Sequence: replace the pattern, run it to enumerate, fix all 59, land as one commit. Do not repair the pattern in isolation. Closed 2026-08-13 on branch claude/pr-review-708-o1u2rr: the inline bash -c entry is replaced by scripts/check_no_em_dash.py (locale-independent, matches the literal character) wired with pass_filenames so it scans the staged change rather than the whole index, which was the row's second latent defect: one legacy violation under git grep --cached -- . would have failed every later commit. This row's mechanism analysis is independently confirmed: 62 occurrences across 18 files at LC_ALL=C against 0 at LC_ALL=C.UTF-8, up from the 59 measured 2026-08-11. All 50 prose occurrences across 7 files are fixed; the 5 load-bearing ones (raw.count in two evidence scripts, three assert ... not in in test_report_retention.py) carry an em-dash-ok marker plus a reason; generated artifacts under out/ are excluded in both the hook and the guard test, following the PR #708 precedent of leaving generated output as generated. Regression coverage: tests/unit/test_check_no_em_dash.py (11 tests) asserts detection fires and that the tree stays clean. CI hygiene done
    UW-F32 Four Postgres majors are in play at once, with no stated policy. Found 2026-08-13 while checking test/production parity. Production runs 17.6 (Supabase, read via the read-only connector); tests/integration/conftest.py ran 16 via testcontainers and is bumped to 17 on branch claude/pr-review-708-o1u2rr to track the production major, since every schema-parity and RLS assertion in that suite was otherwise measuring a different engine than the one it makes claims about; docker-compose.yml runs dhi-postgres:16-debian13; and e2e-real-nightly.yml / e2e-real-pr-smoke.yml run 18, digest-pinned, one major AHEAD of production.     UW-F37 DeviceAuthorizedRoute can resurrect a revoked device grant from the IndexedDB mirror. Found 2026-08-15 by Greptile during review of PR #720, in code that PR does not touch (frontend/src/auth/DeviceAuthorizedRoute.tsx, unchanged since v0.70.0 / #660). Sequence: another tab revokes, clearing localStorage and firing clearDeviceGrantMirror(), which is fire-and-forget; before the queued storage event reaches this tab, the visitor follows the still-rendered /kids link; the route's own mount effect sees no localStorage grant, hydrates, finds the surviving mirror, restores it, and renders the kid surface for a device whose access was taken away.
