    UW-F38 A flowed stop loses its origin node's prose on any remount, so every book at 8-11 and up silently drops the opening passage of each stop. Found 2026-08-15 while diagnosing the 28-day-red e2e-real-nightly tier; reproduced against a real local stack (Postgres + seeded uvicorn), not inferred from a spec. ADR-026 flows consecutive single-choice, non-ending nodes into one rendered stop at the flowed bands (FLOWED_BANDS in frontend/src/reader/readerProgress.ts: 8-11, 10-13, 13-16, 16+). useFlowedStop composes the stop from the origin, renders every node's body joined, then silently walks the engine to the stop's TERMINAL via real CHOOSE events (ADR-026 decision 2, so effects and visit_set apply). That advanced state is persisted. useFlowedStop holds the origin only in component state (useState<Tracked \| null>(null)), so any remount starts from the persisted terminal and composeStop returns a length-1 stop. Instrumented proof on s_dev_ember_1 (band 10-13) from a truncated reading_state: two correct composes at origin=n_e1_start nodeIds=[n_e1_start,n_e1_decision1], then a third at origin=n_e1_decision1 nodeIds=[n_e1_decision1]. Rendered passage-body was Ember Trail 1: a fork in the path demands a choice. alone; the start node's Ember Trail 1: the trail begins. never reached the screen, while the persisted row correctly read current_node=n_e1_decision1, path=[n_e1_start, n_e1_decision1]. The engine is right and the render is wrong, which is why no backend test catches it. Reader-visible impact, not just CI: a child who closes and reopens any flowed-band book resumes at the terminal and never sees the prose that set the scene. It also means five nightly specs fail for this one cause (series-continue-real x2, kid-read-aloud-real, kid-go-back-real, offline-online-parity-real, plus offline-reconnect-real's conflict case). Not fixed here, deliberately: the fix changes shared reader semantics under an ADR (reconstruct the stop origin by walking back over the persisted path while each predecessor has exactly one satisfied choice, versus persisting the origin, versus storing the stop origin in reading state), and picking one is an owner call rather than a test repair. Fixed 2026-08-15: composeStopWithHistory / flowedPrefix (player/stops.ts) re-derive the prefix from the recorded path (structural, no condition re-evaluation, fails closed to today's behavior on any ambiguity), and useFlowedStop now walks the silent CHOOSE batch forward from the ENGINE's position rather than the stop's first node, so a reconstructed prefix is rendered without its hops being re-applied. Widening nodeIds also repairs Go back on a resumed read, since backOneStop calls back() once per node in the stop. Covered by 8 new cases in stops.test.ts and a new useFlowedStop.test.ts; the shared stop-trace conformance corpus still passes on both sides. The affected specs were then corrected to stop tapping single-choice nodes, which are correctly never rendered at a flowed band. Landed in #724 (9877fdb, hardened by 50ea8eb and 5491631). 4b done
    UW-F39 An offline read of a flowed stop syncs one transition short, and the app reports itself fully caught up. Found 2026-08-15 while closing UW-F38; reproduced on a real local stack. In offline-online-parity-real.spec.ts's offline walk, tapping c_climb on s_clockwork_garden (band 10-13) enters n_tower, whose single onward choice flows straight into the ending, and the UI correctly shows the ending screen with the right ending-id. On reconnect the app raises its own "All caught up! Your reading is saved." toast, which ReaderRoute.tsx::handleReplayOutcome only fires when every queued write replayed cleanly, yet the server row is still at n_tower, and stays there when polled for a further 10s. The ONLINE half of the same walk reaches n_clock_end correctly once given time to settle, so this is specific to the offline queue rather than to flowed stops generally. Two candidate mechanisms, neither confirmed: the flowed transition's save is never enqueued while offline, or both transitions are enqueued against the same state_revision and the second is dropped as stale on replay without surfacing as a conflict. The second would be the more serious, because a silently-dropped write that still reports success is the exact failure the 409 machinery exists to make visible. Sequenced after UW-F38, not with it: the online half of this spec is green, so this is now the only thing that spec reports, and it is a sync-layer question rather than a rendering one. Closed 2026-08-24 by #761. NEITHER candidate mechanism was right, and the second one, the more serious of the two, is specifically ruled out: the flowed transition IS enqueued, and no write is dropped as stale without surfacing. Instrumented against a real local stack (every reading-state PUT logged with node, base revision, and status). The real cause is a THIRD writer nobody had counted. saveProgress always tried the network first and enqueued only on an OfflineError, so a choice made just before connectivity returned went out as a LIVE write while older writes for the same row were still queued. On reconnect the live write and replayQueue hit the same row in the same millisecond, both carrying the same base revision (nothing can advance state_revision while offline). One was accepted, the other 409'd, and that 409 latched conflicted, so every remaining queued write was held and reported as a conflict. Which side loses is a race, and the dangerous direction is when the LIVE write loses: ReaderPage adopts the replayed older row and remounts, rewinding the child to a node they had already left. One inconsistency in this row is deliberately left standing rather than papered over: the toast and the latched conflict cannot both belong to the same drain. ReaderRoute.tsx::handleReplayOutcome raises "All caught up" only when o.replayed > 0 && o.conflicts.length === 0 && o.failed.length === 0, so a toast asserts a clean drain, while the mechanism above has the first 409 latching conflicted and holding every remaining write, which reports conflicts and suppresses the toast. An earlier version of this row read the suppression rule as the REASON the toast appeared, which inverts it. Which half is mis-recorded is not established, and this row cannot settle it: the instrumentation was run against offline-reconnect-real.spec.ts, not against the parity spec whose toast produced this row's title, and the parity spec's offline half was verified green rather than separately instrumented (see the next sentence, which always said so). The FIX is unaffected either way, having been measured directly on the instrumented spec; what is unsettled is the account of the symptom that named the row. Re-running the parity spec's offline half with per-outcome logging would settle it, and is carried by UW-F45's double-audit sweep. Fix: a live write appends behind a non-empty queue for the same row instead of overtaking it, and replayQueue drains in passes so a write appended mid-drain is not stranded until an online event that may never come. Measured directly on offline-reconnect-real.spec.ts; the parity spec's offline half was fixed by the same change and verified green rather than separately instrumented. Genuine cross-device conflict semantics are unchanged, still proven by the sibling test at offline-reconnect-real.spec.ts:229. 4b done
    UW-F40 Go back does not revert the persisted reading-state row. kid-go-back-real.spec.ts walks s_tide_pools (band 8-11) n_start -> n_pools -> n_crab, taps Go back, and asserts the PUT it triggers saves current_node = n_pools; the row comes back n_crab, i.e. the position the child just left. Pre-existing and independent of UW-F38: the identical expected/received pair was recorded in the first diagnostic run of this tier, before any reader change, and the fix for UW-F38 neither improved nor worsened it. Not a flowed-prefix artifact either: n_pools and n_crab both offer two choices, so each is its own length-1 stop and backOneStop reduces to a single back() call here. That leaves the interesting possibilities: back() failing its replay contract and the reader persisting the unchanged state anyway, or the reader saving before applying the rewind. Worth reading back()'s fail-closed replay rule (engine.ts::replayRecordedPath, which accepts only a replay whose terminal var_state equals the live one) against s_tide_pools's recorded path first, since that is the one path in this walk that now begins at n_open rather than n_start. Closed 2026-08-24 by #761. BOTH candidate mechanisms were wrong, and so was this row's title: go back always reverted the persisted row. The engine was exonerated by direct simulation over the real seeded s_tide_pools blob, which is the check this row asked for: back() and backOneStop() both returned n_pools with path [n_open, n_start, n_pools], and the stop is length 1 so exactly one BACK is sent. Logging every PUT in the real browser then showed the go-back save itself was always correct. The actual cause is at MOUNT. persist() stamps state_revision from revisionRef, which only advances when a save RESPONSE lands, so the two saves ADR-026 emits while flowing the opening stop (one per node walked, before the child touches anything) both stamped the same revision; the second was rejected, the 409 branch adopted the server row and remounted the Reader, and the remount replayed the same two emissions. Self-sustaining, not transient: opening s_tide_pools sent 85 PUTs in the first two seconds and did not stop until the child made a choice. That churn desynchronised the spec's wait-then-act pairing, so the PUT it captured after the go-back tap was the PREVIOUS choice's n_crab save. Not a dev-only artifact: measured against a production vite build. Fix: chain each save behind the previous one, advancing the chain slot synchronously before the first await. After it the same walk sends 5 PUTs, all 200, revisions strictly increasing. The same loop was also the detached-DOM click churn that failed offline-online-parity-real.spec.ts:270 for 30s, which is why one fix cleared three reported failures. Two stale path assertions in the spec (expecting ['n_start', 'n_pools'], predating the PL-25 n_open prelude) were corrected in the same commit. This closed the MOUNT revision-stamping race only; the nightly kept failing for a second, distinct spec-level cause after this fix landed, see UW-F48. 4b done
    UW-F41 e2e-prod has been red since 2026-08-05 on every AUTHENTICATED adult console route, and the cause is not determinable from the repository. #623. Established 2026-08-15 from the run-31888348727 job log. Clean split: every UNAUTHENTICATED assertion passes (landing page, sign-in form fields, the unauthenticated redirect, the no-Apple-sign-in check, and all three health probes, including the canonical readiness endpoint answering as real FastAPI JSON), while /guardian, /guardian/books, /guardian/profiles all fail on a missing <h1> and the kid device-grant spec times out at 45s. Not diagnosable from here and not fixed: this environment cannot reach cyo.williamshome.family (the agent proxy refuses the host) and cannot download the run's trace artifact (session GitHub policy), and the traces expire after 7 days, so eleven scheduled runs have produced no retrievable evidence. That converts this from a blind failure into a self-answering one. Owner action: read the next run's alert comment; it should name the cause directly. The diagnostics landed above did exactly what they were added for. The final red run (32643763182, 2026-08-23) reports every failing console assertion at url: https://cyo.williamshome.family/guardian/unavailable, lazy route fallback still mounted: false, and body text "We can't reach CYO Adventure ... That is GuardianBackendUnavailablePage, which ProtectedRoute routes to (rather than to login) when the authenticated bootstrap call fails. The production API was not answering the authenticated adult bootstrap, and the frontend reported that honestly. Green since: 2026-08-24 and 2026-08-25 both pass, with no repository change between the last red and the first green, which puts the trigger and the recovery on the production side (a redeploy is the obvious candidate; see UW-A03 and the deploy-currency oracles in the runbook). Residual, which is why this is verify and not done: nothing has established WHY the backend stopped answering, so the same outage can recur unrecognised; and issue #623 is still open with its last comment dated 2026-08-23.     UW-F42 A resumed read applies a saved reading-state to whatever story VERSION the route loads, with no check that the two agree. Raised by Greptile on #724 and confirmed against the code. ReaderPage.tsx loads the story at a route-selected version (getCachedStorybook(storybookId, version) / fetchStory(storybookId, version)) but loads the reading state by (profileId, storybookId) alone: neither getReadingState nor fetchServerState takes a version, and nothing compares them before saved becomes initialReading. So a republish can hand the player a current_node, path and var_state recorded against a different topology. Pre-existing, not introduced by #724: the reader already applied a stale-version current_node to the newly loaded story, and back() already replayed a stale path; that PR only added a third reader of path (flowedPrefix). Partially mitigated there: flowedPrefix now returns [] unless state.version === story.version, so stop reconstruction refuses a cross-version path outright rather than relying on the structural guards to catch it. That closes the reconstruction surface and nothing else. Still open: the resume itself. back()'s fail-closed replay (it accepts only a replay whose terminal var_state equals the live one) blunts the worst case, but a node id that still exists in the new version with different prose or different choices is resumed silently. To decide: whether a version mismatch should discard the saved state and start fresh, attempt a best-effort migration, or prompt. Discarding is the honest default and costs a child their place; the others are worse in different ways, which is why this is a ruling rather than a fix. Note the data needed is already present and unused: ReadingState.version is stamped by engine.ts on every transition, and the API returns it. Owner to rule: the project owner. 4b decision
    UW-F43 composeStopWithHistory changes what a tap out of a loop-terminated stop renders, and its own comment says it cannot. Found 2026-08-16 reviewing #724. useFlowedStop states that outside a resume "the predecessor is a real branch (or there is no predecessor) so the prefix is empty and this is exactly composeStop". That holds for three of the four terminal reasons and fails for loop, whose terminal has exactly ONE visible choice and is therefore tappable. Traced against the shared corpus fixture schema/conformance/stop_traces.json::loop_back_ends_stop (s_stop_loop, band 10-13, so a flowed band; n_q's only choice targets n_p): after the fresh stop [n_p, n_q] closes on the loop guard, tapping c_to_p gives path=[n_p, n_q, n_p], flowedPrefix returns ['n_q'], and the composed stop becomes nodeIds=['n_q','n_p'] with the engine left at n_p. Three visible consequences: the prose renders n_q (which the child just read) ahead of n_p; startIndex resolves to the last index so NO CHOOSE is sent and n_q's on_enter never re-fires, which is exactly the counter a loop_and_grow story exists to grow; and choiceState becomes n_p, so the child is offered the other room's choice. Before this change the same tap produced [n_p, n_q] with the engine at n_q. Not reachable in publishable content, and that is the whole finding. A closed single-choice cycle is an SCC with no exit edge, which validator/layer1.py::_check_trap_loops rejects as L1-5; confirmed empirically, the only flowed-band fixture with this shape is tests/fixtures/storybook/invalid/graph/trap_loop.json, and every loop_and_grow skeleton in the catalog sits at 3-5 or 5-8, both non-flowed. So no child can hit it today. But flowedPrefix's docstring promises that any ambiguity "stops the walk and yields the shorter prefix, so the worst case is today's behavior rather than a wrong one", and here the worst case is a wrong one: the guarantee is being carried by a validator rule the reader neither references nor can see. Why it is not fixed here: the obvious gates each break a legitimate case. Gating reconstruction on tracked === null (resume only) breaks Go back, which lands on the previous stop's terminal and genuinely needs the prefix. Refusing any prefix that closes a cycle also breaks the resumed-cycle case stops.test.ts already pins ('ends a resumed cycle where it originally ended'), because the two are indistinguishable from flowedPrefix's inputs alone: both see a single-choice predecessor and a path that revisits. The discriminator has to come from the caller, which knows whether the engine moved forward (a tap) or backward (Go back) or not at all (a mount), most likely by comparing reading.path.length against the tracked stop's. That is a reader-semantics decision under ADR-026, so it wants an owner call and a test for each of the three entries rather than a guess. 4b unscheduled
    UW-F44 modal.py cannot recognise a token-budget exhaustion, so it retries one deterministically. ModalProvider._extract_completion guards empty content correctly (if not content: catches both None and "") but raises it as leg_fatal=False, and it never digs finish_reason. run_with_retries therefore reissues the identical request at the identical max_tokens with exponential backoff before failing as exhausted, burning the whole retry budget on calls that cannot succeed. openrouter.py already implements the correct treatment (leg_fatal=finish_reason == "length", with the cause worded into the error); port it. Second item in the same change: dig_reasoning_tokens reads only OpenRouter's nested usage.completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens and modal.py never calls it, so the Modal leg collects no reasoning telemetry at all and budget sizing cannot be driven from measurements. Measured and evidenced in handoff-modal-deepseek-v4-smoke-test-2026-08-20.md. Experimental-only per ADR-010, so this gates real Modal use rather than production today. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F45 The reader suite's shared SyncApi double accepts every revision, so no unit test there can see an optimistic-concurrency defect. Found 2026-08-24 while closing UW-F39 and UW-F40, which were both revision-stamping races. ReaderPage.test.tsx's okApi() returns {status: 200} for every putReadingState whatever state_revision the body carries, and it backs most of that file's ~50 tests. The real PUT /v1/reading-state rejects a mismatched revision with a 409, so an entire class of defect (two writers stamping the same base) was structurally invisible to the unit tier and only ever surfaced in e2e-real, where it presented as three unrelated-looking failures across three specs. #761 adds one concurrency-enforcing double alongside okApi() rather than replacing it, because most tests there are not about revisions and the permissive double keeps them readable. The work here is the sweep that commit deliberately did not do: audit the other reader and offline doubles (src/offline/sync.test.ts, ReaderRoute.test.tsx, AuthContext.test.tsx) for the same shape, and decide which of them should enforce the precondition their real endpoint enforces. General principle worth carrying past this row: a test double that silently accepts a precondition the real dependency enforces converts a whole defect class into a green suite. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F46 Nothing detects that the scheduled-failure alerting itself has stopped working, and its failure mode is silence. Fourteen schedule-only workflows now delegate their only notification channel to one composite action (.github/actions/ci-failure-issue), which is a real improvement on eleven drifted copies: the fleet-wide invariants are executable now (tests/unit/test_ci_failure_action_contract.py) and the script's behaviour runs against Octokit doubles on every push (.github/actions/ci-failure-issue/test/reconcile.test.mjs, the alert-action leg of ci-gate). It also concentrates the risk: a defect that survives both, or a GITHUB_TOKEN whose issues: write scope is revoked by an org policy change, silences all fourteen at once, and no signal distinguishes that from a fortnight with nothing wrong. Issue #667 is the measured cost of this shape at one-workflow scale: sixteen consecutive nightly backups cancelled at the concurrency gate, zero jobs dispatched, no failure reported, and seventeen days before anyone noticed. Two of the three legs that gap is now closed on are new in #771; the third, liveness, is not, and cannot be tested from inside the thing being monitored. Proposed: a heartbeat that asserts the mechanism ran rather than that it reported nothing. Cheapest credible version is a weekly workflow that files and immediately resolves a [canary] issue through the same action and fails if either leg does not land, which exercises the token scope, the assignment path, and the lookup against the real API. That is a canary, not a monitor: it proves the action works on the day it runs, and does not prove any particular workflow's cron is alive. Whether per-workflow cron liveness is worth a second mechanism (a dead-man's-switch per marker) is an owner call and explicitly out of scope here. Deferred from #771's review as too large for that PR and requiring a decision it should not make unilaterally. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F47 A #VERIFY marker inside .github/ is invisible to the RAD citation gate, so it can name a test that no longer exists and nothing fails. scripts/check_rad_citations.py "only ever parses .py, .ts and .tsx" by its own docstring, and the check-rad-citations hook's files: pattern admits only four things (Python under src/, Python under scripts/, .ts/.tsx under frontend/src/, and the baseline TOML), so it excludes .github/ entirely. That was harmless while no marker lived there. #771 put the first load-bearing ones in .github/actions/ci-failure-issue/action.yml: its #EDGE concurrency marker cites tests/unit/test_ci_failure_action_contract.py for two invariants (no call site in a multi-leg matrix job, every call site's workflow carries a concurrency: group) precisely so they stop being prose, and its #CRITICAL external resources marker points at the assignee-drop assertions. Those citations resolve today, verified by hand at merge. The gap is that renaming or deleting either test leaves a marker asserting a guarantee nobody checks, which is worse than an uncited assumption because it reads as verified. Proposed: extend the checker to parse # comments in .github/**/*.yml (YAML shares Python's comment character, so the marker grammar already applies) and widen the hook's files: pattern to match, then re-baseline. Scope note: the hook's own comment already records the same blindness for supabase/ and frontend/e2e/, so the fix should widen the gate once for all three trees rather than special-casing .github/. Surfaced while reviewing #771. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F48 The nightly real-backend E2E failed 31 consecutive nights, 2026-07-28 through 2026-08-27 (last success 2026-07-27), of which two (2026-08-26, 2026-08-27) fell after UW-F40's fix (#761, landed 2026-08-25 15:24 UTC) for a second, distinct cause UW-F40 did not touch. #761 fixed a revision-stamping race at MOUNT (chained saves). It never touched kid-go-back-real.spec.ts's own waitForReadingStatePut, which (as of 259421fa, #369) matched a reading-state PUT by URL and method only, never by the request body's own current_node. f68c4f71 (#635) later added the n_open prelude that makes mount emit a second PUT where it emitted one, shifting every subsequent order-blind wait by one queue position: the predicate could resolve on a still-in-flight earlier save instead of the one the calling click had just triggered. A decisive experiment (6 of 6 runs) established the server's persisted state was correct throughout every failure; this was a spec defect, not a recurrence of UW-F40's product bug, and not covered by UW-F45's double-fidelity finding either (this tier hits the real backend, no double involved). Fix written in dca131cb (matches every waitForReadingStatePut call by request body current_node, not queue position), but as of 2026-08-28 that commit is not on main: it exists only on local branch fix/testing-ladder-trust (dca131cb is not an ancestor of origin/main, and the branch has no open pull request), so the nightly failed again on 2026-08-28 (33175028072) with the identical n_pools/n_crab signature at the same assertion, for the same already-diagnosed cause, extending the streak past the 31 nights this row originally recorded. Status is blocked, not done: the register's status vocabulary reserves done for a claim backed by a PR/commit/issue reference that has actually landed, and verify for genuine uncertainty about whether something is already resolved; neither fits a case where the branch state and the nightly's continued failure are both already confirmed. The named blocker: open a pull request from fix/testing-ladder-trust (or cherry-pick dca131cb onto a branch off main) and merge it. Do not flip this back to done until dca131cb (or an equivalent fix) is verified reachable from origin/main and the nightly has gone green.     UW-F50 RESOLVED 2026-08-28. The 3 failures in frontend/e2e/reader-flag.spec.ts were a pre-existing test-fixture gap, not a fix/testing-ladder-trust regression, and the gap is now closed. Root cause: ReaderRoute/ReaderPage fire four calls on mount that this spec's beforeEach never mocked (GET /v1/profiles, GET /v1/me/progress, PUT /v1/device-downloads, GET /v1/characters). On a machine where something answers on :8000, those calls fall through vite preview's /api proxy to that real backend and get a genuine 401 for the spec's fabricated bearer; useApi's response interceptor reads a 401 on a request tagged as carrying the child token as "the session died" and calls clearChildSession(), which hides the FlagButton these tests click, so the verdict inverted on whether an unrelated process happened to be bound to :8000 rather than on the flag flow itself. In CI, nothing listens on :8000, so the same calls 502 instead, the interceptor's 401 branch never fires, and the spec passes; verified against the last two non-skipped main runs and reproduced directly both ways (a live backend on :8000 gives the exact 3-failed/1-passed shape; the same build's preview pointed at a guaranteed-dead port instead passes 4/4). Fixed by commit c0cbea2f (mocking all four endpoints in the spec's own beforeEach), proven hermetic by running the fixed spec to 4/4 against both a reachable :8000 backend and a dead one. Correction to this row's citation. This row previously cited b2d1a3da, the pre-amend commit that c0cbea2f's amend folded into itself; b2d1a3da is not reachable from HEAD (git merge-base --is-ancestor b2d1a3da HEAD fails) and survives only as an expiring reflog entry, so it stops resolving once ordinary git gc reclaims it. c0cbea2f is the commit that actually landed and is reachable today, so the citation is corrected to it. The other seven reader/library-tier specs the webkit-kid project also selects (reader.spec.ts, reader-conflict.spec.ts, reader-go-back.spec.ts, reader-reload-resume.spec.ts, series-continue.spec.ts, library.spec.ts, kid-read-aloud.spec.ts) were audited for the same hole and do not share it today: none of them currently seeds a real child_session in its beforeEach (only this file does), so the interceptor's session-clearing branch currently has nothing to clear regardless of what those four endpoints return; confirmed by running all 40 tests across the eight specs against both a live :8000 backend and a dead one, all 40 passing both times, both before and after this fix (the fix touched only this one file). This immunity is contingent, not structural, and must not be read as unconditional. The gating mechanism is useApi.ts's response interceptor (frontend/src/hooks/useApi.ts, around lines 257-266): a request is tagged as child-bearing, the precondition for a 401 to clear the session, only when getValidChildSession() returns non-null at issue time. Any of the other seven specs reacquires this exact hole the moment it starts seeding a real child_session, with no change to itself and no signal to whoever makes that change that the precondition it was relying on has shifted. A future author adding child_session seeding to one of those seven must re-derive this audit for that spec rather than assume the "do not share it" conclusion above still holds; see the matching note in frontend/e2e/reader-flag.spec.ts's file header, placed next to the mocks that exist because of this exact mechanism. A shared "mock these four" helper was considered and rejected: the eight specs' beforeEach blocks genuinely differ in what they need (six mock none of the four at all and remain correct without them; library.spec.ts already mocks GET /v1/profiles alone, for its own KidNav assertions, not to avoid this defect), so a shared fixture would either mock endpoints most callers do not need or reduce to per-spec configuration that just re-implements what each file already has. Correction to this row's original evidence. This row originally claimed the spec and five dependency files were byte-identical to origin/main, verified by an empty three-dot diff; that was true when the row was written (2026-08-27) and is false now. ef24e563 landed on this branch afterward and changed this spec's GET /v1/reading-state/** mock from a 404 envelope to 200 {"state": null}. Re-derived directly: git diff origin/main...HEAD --stat -- frontend/e2e/reader-flag.spec.ts frontend/e2e/support/auth.ts frontend/e2e/support/fixtures.ts frontend/src/reader/FlagButton.tsx frontend/src/reader/ReaderPage.tsx frontend/src/reader/Reader.tsx frontend/src/reader/ReaderRoute.tsx frontend/src/hooks/useApi.ts frontend/src/kid/useKidProfile.ts, run immediately before this fix landed, showed exactly one file changed (reader-flag.spec.ts, 1 insertion/1 deletion), and every other file in that list, including FlagButton.tsx, useApi.ts, ReaderRoute.tsx, and useKidProfile.ts, was still byte-identical to origin/main. That one line is the reading-state envelope change, unrelated to the four endpoints that actually drove this failure; it neither caused nor altered the failure. The row's original conclusion (the failure predates the branch) still holds even though the evidence it originally cited for that conclusion no longer does. Separately, the investigation surfaced a real product question about useApi's interceptor clearing the child session on a 401 from any kid-token-route call, not only the one gating the current screen; recorded as its own decision row, UW-F52, rather than folded in here. 5 done
    UW-F51 Eight scheduled workflows have no failure signal of any kind, and a ninth alerts on findings but is silent when it crashes. Found 2026-08-27 while converting e2e-staging.yml to the shared composite action. UW-F46 covers the risk that the alerting mechanism itself dies quietly; this row covers workflows that never had one. Verified per workflow by grepping each for issues: write, createIssue, gh issue, ci-failure-issue, and slack/webhook/notify/alert/mail, with all probes returning empty: container-security.yml (0 7 * * 3), fips-compatibility.yml (0 10 * * 1), python-compatibility.yml (0 6 * * 0), sbom.yml (0 8 * * 1), scorecard.yml (21 21 * * 2), security-analysis.yml (7 9 * * *), validate-cruft.yml (0 9 * * 1), and e2e-staging.yml (0 13 * * *, closed on this branch). Three of the remaining seven are security controls (container-security, security-analysis, scorecard), where a scan that stops running and says nothing is indistinguishable from a scan that keeps passing. The ninth is a distinct and more interesting case, and an earlier version of this finding got it wrong. dependency-provenance-weekly.yml (23 6 * * 1) was first counted among the unalerted because it contains no in-repo alerting; it in fact delegates to the org reusable workflow ByronWilliamsCPA/.github/.github/workflows/python-dependency-provenance.yml (pinned 6b521336) and passes it issues: write, and that workflow does file and update a tracking issue. The error came from grepping only for the in-repo composite action, which cannot see alerting a called workflow performs; delegated alerting is invisible to single-repo analysis and needs the pinned SHA's content fetched to resolve. What the fetch then showed is the real finding: that reusable workflow contains no failure()-gated step at all, and its issue-filing path is gated on hashFiles('dependency-provenance-report.md') != ''. So a run that finds vulnerabilities files an issue, and a run that crashes before writing the report files nothing, which is the same report-when-it-works, silent-when-it-breaks shape this branch keeps meeting. Proposed: convert the seven in-repo workflows to .github/actions/ci-failure-issue as e2e-staging.yml now is, sequenced by blast radius with the three security controls first. The ninth is deliberately NOT proposed here: fixing it edits ByronWilliamsCPA/.github, which every consuming repo inherits, so it is a cross-repo owner decision rather than work this branch may take unilaterally. 5 unscheduled
    UW-F52 Product observation, not a defect: useApi's response interceptor clears the child session on a 401 from ANY kid-token-route request that carried it, not only the one the current screen is actually waiting on. Surfaced while diagnosing and fixing UW-F50. ReaderRoute/ReaderPage fire several calls incidental to reading itself on mount (a profile-name lookup, a progress-settings fetch, a device-download report, an active-character lookup) alongside the reading-state call that actually matters to the page; useApi.ts's response interceptor classifies a 401 by whether the failing request carried the child bearer (the usedChildToken branch), not by which endpoint failed or whether that endpoint is load-bearing for what the child is doing right now. On a real device, a single transient 401 from, say, the character lookup, would sign a child out of the story they are actively reading even though the reading-state call itself succeeded and the story is otherwise fine. This may well be the intended fail-safe for a children's product: treat any rejection of the child's credential as reason to distrust the whole session rather than risk serving content under a token the server has started rejecting. This row does not propose changing that behavior and no code was changed in service of it (useApi.ts's interceptor was deliberately left untouched); the point is only that nobody appears to have decided this on purpose, it is a side effect of classifying by "did this bearer fail" rather than "did the specific call this screen depends on fail". Unverified against real device behaviour: this is traced from the client source (useApi.ts, childSession.ts) and from a mocked Playwright reproduction, not observed on real hardware under a flaky network; a transient 401 from an incidental endpoint in the wild may be rarer or more common than the reader-mount case that surfaced it. Owner (product/security) to rule on whether to keep the blanket clear-on-any-401 behavior as a deliberate fail-safe, or narrow session-clearing to the request(s) the current screen actually depends on. 5 decision
    UW-F53 Human labelling of the C1 comprehension-probe pilot's findings, which nothing previously staged. UW-C423/AL-688 are about background-process spend observability and stage no labelling; the C1 report's claim that labelling was staged was false, and this row is the correction. The probe's unlabelled_unanswerable_rate is explicitly not a precision figure: it counts questions the answer model said it could not answer from the passage, which conflates a genuinely ambiguous passage with an answer model that missed something stated plainly. Turning it into a quality signal requires a human to read every recorded finding and judge which it was, then a decision on whether the rate is fit to gate anything. Inputs are durable as of 191847a9: each run's aggregate (rate, per-story breakdown, model ids, prompt version, corpus slice, SAMPLING STRATEGY, observed spend) is written to out/reports/comprehension-probe/summary-<run_id>.json, tracked, with latest-summary.json beside it as a pointer that carries no findings of its own (AL-694: the fixed name it replaces meant the second paid run destroyed the first run's aggregate, and it did). The run to label is summary-20260829T002840Z.json; the raw questions and answers stay under the gitignored tmp/comprehension-probe-reports/<run>/stories/ and MUST be labelled from there before that directory is cleared, or the run has to be paid for again. Until this is done, no promote-or-reject verdict rests on the rate and it must not be quoted as a quality bar. Amended 2026-08-29: the earlier, superseded depth-interleaved pilot ($0.0209 observed, 119 calls, rate 0.1954) is the only empirical support this branch has for the opening-prefix bias claim (0.1954 prefix-sampled vs 0.1356 stride-sampled, same corpus, same models, same prompt version). It was deliberately NOT republished as a tracked aggregate once the stride-sampled fix landed, and that call was correct: a superseded rate re-added to out/reports/ would read as a second live finding rather than as the comparison point it is. It survives only at 804e8edb:out/reports/comprehension-probe/latest-summary.json (git show that ref-and-path), with its raw findings at tmp/comprehension-probe-reports/20260828T234737Z/; this clause is the only pointer to either, so it must not be deleted. 5 unscheduled

    UW-G19 Generation cost and latency instrumentation, as a prerequisite to any architecture comparison. Correction 2026-08-23: the premise below was true when written and is now false, and the row's own text was the thing that had gone stale. The money half shipped on 2026-08-11 (migration 20260811160000_add_generation_job_provider_accounting.sql): generation_job now carries input_tokens, output_tokens, provider_call_count, provider_unknown_calls, provider_duration_ms, cost_usd and cost_complete, written by generation/worker.py::_stamp_provider_accounting off a per-job UsageLedger, priced through core/pricing.py, with cost_complete marking a lower bound rather than letting an unpriced call read as free. Cost claims about a generation run are therefore measurements now, subject to that flag. The original premise text follows for provenance: "the generation job record carries status, model, provider, prompt version and report, and carries no token counts, no cost and no duration, so every cost claim we could make today is an estimate presented as a measurement." Residual, which is what keeps this row open: job wall-clock duration as distinct from provider_duration_ms (which the column's own docstring says is not the job's runtime), retry count, repair count, and validator-failure count. Human review minutes came off this list on 2026-08-23, one day after the correction above was written: #746 added the submitted pipeline event and publishing/gate_metrics.py, which pairs gate entry with released/sent_back and reports median_duration_seconds, send_back_rate and mean_rounds_to_release. That measurement is PROSPECTIVE only, by design: a decision with no preceding entry is dropped rather than paired with a neighbour, and every pre-migration released row is such a row, so there is no historical review-minutes baseline and D3's notional rate is what the model has to use until observations accumulate. The derived-measures page is step 4's R-5, not this row. Record per run, split by what is already recorded so the list stops reading as wholly outstanding: input and output tokens, cost and final status are columns on generation_job today; human review minutes is measured prospectively by the submitted gate entry described above and is no longer sought here; wall-clock duration, retry count, repair count and validator failures are the residual this row stays open for. Derived measures: cost per candidate, per validator-passing candidate, per approved book, cost of failed books, repair as a percentage of generation, review minutes per approved book, and cost by band and graph size. This is generation operations telemetry and contains no child content, so it sits outside the S10 boundary entirely. Raised by the third external review, which correctly refuses to rank the candidate architectures without it. external-review-3 content unscheduled
    UW-G20 Privacy-minimized all-pairs novelty ledger. The owner has ruled there is no recency window: a child may read any two books in their library back to back, so novelty must hold pairwise across the whole library rather than against recent history. Persist only what a novelty screen needs (pseudonymous profile, book version, experienced node ids, mechanic, device and solution-rule identifiers, ending, first and last seen day) and explicitly not fine-grained timestamps, hesitation, latency, guardian-facing choice-by-choice replay, or story prose. Keep the per-request cost sublinear with inverted indexes over device, solution rule, premise family, cognitive operation and mechanic-sequence hash rather than pairwise evaluation.     UW-G26 Split the random-walk floor by form and enforce it: 11 selectable shells breach it and five give a random-walking reader exactly zero probability of a satisfying ending. Measured across the 84 real graphs. Eight shells print 0.0000 at four decimal places, meaning at or below 0.00005: the-labyrinth-of-glass (13-16), the-thornwood-trial (13-16), the-iron-spire-trial (13-16), the-tenfold-siege (16+), the-ashfall-expedition (16+), the-drowned-court (16+), the-pale-road (16+) and the-red-meridian-run (16+); five of those are exactly zero and the source table does not identify which five, so re-derive before quoting a per-shell figure. 16+ is gamebook form, where a punishing win rate is a genre convention and the cell median is 0.0000 by design, so the defensible position is not that the floor is too low but that one floor covers two forms and is enforced for neither. Work: make the floor form-aware across scripts/check_skeleton.py, validator/band_profile.py and validator/walk.py; enforce it for prose CYO; write down an explicit lower, non-zero gamebook exemption; and fix or declare that satisfying_walk_probability ignores choice conditions and can return a non-converged estimate. Enforcement runs through the guard battery, so it is gated on UW-C453 giving that battery an invoker. The three 13-16 shells at zero become non-compliant and need repair or retirement; check cell depth first, because retiring them must not empty a cell. AL-735 and UW-C460 carry the lesson. brief-gap plan 2026-08-22, W6 content unscheduled
    UW-G27 Recalibrate CG-1, CG-2 and CG-3 against a non-circular anchor, which blocks every --strict enforcement decision. The CG-* family is calibrated to an internal designer table while the rest of the bar is calibrated to the JHM corpus, and CG-2 demands three-way decisions where JHM's maximum outdegree averages 2.58. It is also the whole of the strict delta: enforcing --strict today collapses selection from 74 shells to 20, one per cell in 16 of 18 cells, and CG-2's lack of a hub exemption removes open_map (2 of 13 pass) and time_cave (0 of 9) from the catalog entirely. The work costs no model spend: run check_skeleton.py over the 40 JHM digraphs the repository already cites and set a two-sided policy, admitting at least a stated share of the anchor corpus and rejecting all of a seeded-defect corpus, which replaces thresholds that are percentiles of the corpus they gate; add a hub exemption to CG-2; then re-measure the strict failure count and decide enforcement on the new number. Two rule defects found alongside belong in the same change: _build_graph collapses parallel edges while max_indegree counts them, so phantom choices satisfy PL-17, PL-25 and PL-26 while the graph stays a tree for PL-18; and every strict-blocking finding currently prints "advisory only", 2,456 of 2,456. Sequencing: this precedes UW-G25's D2 ruling and every other --strict item, including UW-C444, which carries the missing caller plus the caveat that the 97-percent CG-* share rests on an uncommitted harness and must be re-derived.     Cluster J: remediation-plan and console gaps¶

    UW-J01 P7 auto-assign-on-publish, blocked on a requested_by_profile_id migration. Raised independently by the persona audit. Unblocked 2026-08-15: the named blocker does not exist and probably has not since before this row was written. The 2026-07-17 remediation plan recorded it as "auto-assign needs a schema migration threading requested_by_profile_id request -> concept -> storybook (no such link exists)", and this row inherited that wording. The link is complete in the shipped schema, under a different column name than the blocker named, which is the likeliest reason nobody re-checked: story_request.profile_id holds the requesting child profile and story_request.resulting_storybook_id holds the storybook the request produced (both db/models.py), and publishing/service.py::approve already calls _stamp_resulting_storybook_id and emits EventType.RELEASED at the exact point an auto-assign hook would run, so both ends are in scope of one function and no migration is required. Re-raised independently by the 2026-08-14 registration walkthrough (onboarding-flow-walkthrough-2026-08-14.md) as its highest-severity finding: api/library.py:425 hard-requires a storybook_assignment row and api/assignments.py:328 is its only writer, so a guardian who requested a story for a named child still lands that child on an empty shelf. Scheduled as Wave 1 item 1a of onboarding-overhaul-plan-2026-08-15.md, which also carries the three constraints the implementation must reuse rather than reimplement (the K13 age-band ceiling in assign_storybook, assignment idempotency, and the BOOK_ASSIGNED event that drives the notification split in UW-J23).     UW-J23 The story_ready notification claims a shelf the book did not reach. notifications/registry.py:147 composes "It has been approved and published to your family library", which a guardian reads as "it is on the shelf", while the book is assigned to nobody; the correct-sounding sibling composer _compose_book_assigned (line 159) is the one that only fires on a real assignment. Rewrite the published-but-unassigned message to name the remaining step and link to the assign control. Stays worth doing after UW-J01 lands, because auto-assign covers only the single-child request path: catalog books, second children, and profile-less requests still publish unassigned, and a rare wrong message is worse than a common one because nobody is looking for it.     UW-J24 Does an uninvited self-signup stay behind the admin approval gate? Today it does: api/onboarding.py provisions every uninvited subject at status="awaiting_approval" and require_principal then refuses them everything including GET /v1/me, so a brand-new family waits on a platform admin they have no relationship with and no channel to reach, with nothing notifying either party in either direction. Coherent for an invite-shaped deployment; a dead end for anything resembling public signup, which is how the 2026-08-14 walkthrough hit it. The ruling decides the shape of the work, not merely its timing: keeping the gate makes it a notification and queue problem (UW-J29, UW-J30), while opening it makes it a compensating-controls problem, and the guardian-created invite path must keep its gate either way for the reason api/onboarding.py already documents (a guardian can name any address and would otherwise capture a stranger into their family on that person's first sign-in). Sits next to the still-open VPC method decision (UW-N02, UW-A52) and interacts with UW-J25: opening this gate while KWS verification stays off leaves no adulthood check anywhere in the product. Owner to rule: the project owner. Wave 0 item D1 of the onboarding overhaul plan. registration walkthrough 4b decision
    UW-J25 Is kws_verification_required turned on, and does a plain age affirmation ship regardless? The flag defaults to False (core/config.py:1159), so on a default deployment the adulthood-verification step is skipped entirely and the only checks are the self-attestation checkbox on the consent form and the admin gate UW-J24 may remove. The flag flip itself is counsel-gated and already tracked (UW-M03, UW-N02); this row exists to make the combination visible, because the two decisions have never been read together and the unsafe pairing is not obvious from either one alone. The separable half is the age affirmation before the OAuth button (UW-J33), which is not verification, must not be worded as though it were, and needs no counsel ruling. Owners to rule: the project owner with privacy counsel. Wave 0 item D2 of the onboarding overhaul plan. registration walkthrough 4b decision
    UW-J26 frontend/src/guardian/BooksPage.tsx lists published books without distinguishing one on a child's shelf from one on nobody's, so the gap UW-J01 and UW-J23 describe is invisible on the one page a guardian would look at to find it. Add the unassigned state and a direct assign affordance from the list. This is the recovery path for any guardian who already hit the gap, including everyone who hit it before auto-assign shipped, which is why it is not made redundant by UW-J01. Same change adds the existing public /support link to the awaiting-approval screen, which is the one screen in the flow where a stuck guardian currently has nowhere to go and no ETA. Wave 2 item 2a of the onboarding overhaul plan. Widened 2026-08-15, then closed the same day. PR #720 (merged as 5f38917) promoted the same misattribution to the marketing homepage, which grew the row from two console strings to five strings across three files and raised it from a console-copy defect to a claims-accuracy one: the new landing page carried "You approve, then they read" plus "you always have the final word" (an authority a guardian does not hold: api/approval.py requires the admin role on every mutating handler, and api/node_edit.py's docstring states "approval itself stays admin-only regardless"), a trust card titled "A grown-up approves every story", and a footnote promising "Once you approve a book it lands on their shelf right away" (false a second way, see UW-J01: api/library.py requires a storybook_assignment row that publishing never writes). #720's own honesty pass audited claims against ADR-018 and ADR-014 but not against the authorization model, which is why all five survived it. Note the reason these read as true to their authors: on this deployment the single family's adult is dual-role, so "you approve" holds for them and fails for exactly the new families the funnel exists to attract. Closed on branch claude/minor-guardian-registration-flow-o0cmaf: the guardian's two real controls are named instead (passage editing, G6; per-child assignment, G16), the reviewer is named as ours rather than the reader's ("our safety reviewer", the possessive dropped in both the trust card and ConsolePage.tsx), the awaiting-approval copy names who actually acts and stops promising a message nothing sends (that promise is UW-J29), and the /support link this row asked for is on the waiting screen. Pinned by five tests across LandingPage.test.tsx, ConsolePage.test.tsx, and GuardianAwaitingApprovalPage.test.tsx, each asserting the old string's ABSENCE alongside the replacement's presence so deleting the section cannot pass; the two landing assertions were mutation-verified by reintroducing the old copy and confirming they fail. registration walkthrough, PR #720 now done
    UW-J29 Nothing fires when a self-signup lands and nothing fires when it clears. EventType carries USER_MANAGED but no signup event, and notifications/service.py resolves every recipient through a family, so there is no admin-facing delivery path at all; the awaiting-approval page's own 20-second poll is the only feedback in the entire loop.     UW-J37 P2 dashboard overhaul, deferred by design (D1): finding-first detail-page inversion. The review detail page (ReviewDetailPage.tsx) groups review state by node and severity tier, so a reviewer working one finding cannot see its full context (declared vs. observed intensity, node text, prior disposition) in one place. The 2026-08-25 review-process-hardening plan scoped this out to a follow-on plan rather than building it inline, because today's findings carry no stable identity across a re-moderation run: each moderation_report write replaces the findings array wholesale, so nothing lets a reviewer track "this same finding, seen twice" across reports. The named prerequisite is a finding-identity spec that does not exist yet; write it, then invert the page. review-process-hardening plan 2026-08-25, D1 4b blocked
    UW-J38 P2 dashboard overhaul, deferred by design (D1): persistent per-finding triage dispositions. The queue has no per-finding triage state (acknowledged, dismissed, escalated) that survives a session or a re-moderation run; the queue's only metric today is the tiered block/flag/advisory counts from the structural collapse (Task 4 of the same plan), not a durable "N unreviewed" count a reviewer could work down across visits. Shares UW-J37's prerequisite: neither can persist a disposition against a finding that has no stable identity across report rewrites, so this is blocked on the same missing finding-identity spec. review-process-hardening plan 2026-08-25, D1 4b blocked
    UW-J39 P2 dashboard overhaul, deferred by design (D1): declared-vs-observed content-intensity bridging. Nothing in the review UI reconciles the author-declared content flags (violence, scariness, peril, captured at request intake) against what the moderation pipeline actually found in the filled prose. The 2026-08-25 review-process-hardening plan's Task 7 only relabeled the declared flags "Content flags (author-declared)" with a tooltip disclaiming that they are not a moderation verdict, so a reviewer would stop mistaking one for the other; it did not build the comparison. A book whose author declared "mild peril" and whose moderation report found a high-severity violence finding surfaces no mismatch anywhere today. Unlike UW-J37/UW-J38, this does not depend on the finding-identity spec; it is a separate reconciliation feature against existing data. review-process-hardening plan 2026-08-25, D1 4b unscheduled
    UW-J40 Owner-approved follow-up (ratified 2026-08-25): add an admin-only Remoderate action to the review detail page. Auto-remoderation on an unusable report was considered and rejected: it spends LLM budget without a human decision, and a persistent failure would loop. review-process-hardening plan 2026-08-25 4b unscheduled

    UW-K21 No check verifies that an AL-* citation in prose resolves to the lesson it describes. scripts/check_work_linkage.py::_check_lessons_linkage resolves AL-* tokens only where they appear inside cluster C of this register, so a citation anywhere else is never existence-checked and never semantically matched. Found when generation-review-workstream-plan-2026-08-22.md cited AL-551 (the hand-copied-count lesson from PR #738) for the Stage 1 persist-gate defect, which is AL-562 on branch fix/stage1-persist-signal-coupling. The same PR's own step-5 acceptance criterion forbids exactly this ("No AL citation in the brief or in code resolves to the wrong row"), so the criterion currently has no enforcer. Proposed: extend the linkage check to resolve every AL-NNN token under docs/ and src/ against the log, erroring on an ID that does not exist and warning where the cited row's Category does not match the citing context. Two lessons from the 2026-08-23 correction pass are held back until PR #742 merges, because the log requires consecutive IDs and #742 holds AL-562..AL-568: (a) an arithmetic identity over two computations proves nothing until both are shown to range over the same set, and (b) a same-day merge can land a review's subject before the review returns. Unblocked 2026-08-23 by PR #742 merging as 37a08a60, which landed AL-562..AL-575 (not AL-568 as estimated above); the two held-back lessons can now be appended above the current maximum.     UW-K22 GA-D2: restate F5 to say which architecture it means, because D-7b tested the one F5's wording forbids. F5 says never reuse decisions. world_recipe.requires is byte-identical across both D-7b contracts and is a closed, enumerated menu of narrative decisions with draw counts (cipher_forms 1 of 5, access_details 3 of 7, remedies 1 of 6, and six more), so D-7b reuses the decision menu and generates only its realisation. Sharing a closed decision menu across books may well be the right architecture; it is simply not what F5 says. Owner to rule: the project owner, between F5-a, share topology only and generate the decision menu per book, which is expensive and matches the current wording, and F5-b, share topology and the decision menu and generate the realisation per book, which is cheap, matches what D-7b actually tested, and needs a different variety argument because the menu is the thing a reader tracks. Also unreported in the brief and belonging in the F5 decision record: D-7b's recognition verdicts, first-yes position 2 and distinctness 1 of 5, with both raters naming an identical three-way opening act, are the programme's own pre-registered falsifier firing on F5's flagship artifact. Two prerequisites sit in other rows rather than here: UW-C447 voids and re-closes D-6, D-7 and D-7b per section F, and UW-C446 rebuilds the idiom floor from the matched-scale control so every figure citing it can be republished with its interval. Do not reconstruct D-7b selections to run check_solution_transfer.py: tier 1 of that tool is a text-similarity test, so whoever writes the reconstruction sets the answer. doc decision

    Cluster L: live defects¶

    These are not plan gaps. They are bugs found during the sweep, recorded here only so the register is complete; each is filed as a GitHub issue.

    ID Item Issue Status
    UW-L01 machine.ts:108 wires RESTART to start(story), the new-reader entry point, so a restart on a continuation read reseeds var_state from the book's declared initials and returns to story.start_node, discarding both carried series state and the continuation entry node. Root cause: the machine context keeps only {story, reading, error}, dropping the entryNode/varState pair that ReaderPage.tsx:246 passed to startContinuation, so reset cannot reproduce a continuation even in principle. Canonical across ADR-024's deferred defect, debt SL10, diversity B4, and s5-handoff B4. Fixed in #648: ReaderContext now carries the ContinuationSeed, and both the initial-context factory and reset route through one safeStart(story, continuation?), so a restart reproduces the continuation entry node and carried variables. Scope of the close: the machine can now reproduce a continuation, which is what this row said it could not do "even in principle". The seed is still supplied per session from router location.state, so a reload before the first save loses it; that remainder is tracked as the open half of SL10. #460 done
    UW-L02 api/get_db_session commits in the teardown half of a yield-dependency, which FastAPI runs after the response is sent, so a client could act on a 201 whose row no other connection could yet see. Was mitigated only by an 1100 ms newman delay that also serves as rate-limit spacing, so it could not be tuned for the race alone. Fixed in #647, split across two layers because neither can see both facts it needs: middleware/unit_of_work.py commits at the ASGI http.response.start message, which is the last moment before any byte reaches the client and the only place where to commit is observable; the dependency keeps the rollback, because it sits inside ExceptionMiddleware and is the only layer that can tell a raised HTTPException(403) from a deliberately returned JSONResponse(404), which is whether to commit. RequestUnitOfWork settles exactly once so whichever half acts first decides. The dependency's commit survives as the fallback for a request that never emits a response start. #461 verify
    UW-L03 Settings.rate_limit_redis_cooldown_seconds (core/config.py:342, carries a #CRITICAL: timing tag and has unit-test coverage) was never threaded into RateLimitMiddleware, so the middleware fell back to a hardcoded 5.0 and CYO_ADVENTURE_RATE_LIMIT_REDIS_COOLDOWN_SECONDS had zero effect in any deployed environment. Found by the 2026-07-31 vulture calibration. Fixed 2026-07-31 in commit bb42b308 (PR #520, "wire the rate-limit Redis knobs and close the UW-F22 dead-symbol findings"): middleware/security.py:933 now passes _settings.rate_limit_redis_cooldown_seconds into the middleware config, alongside the sibling rate_limit_redis_timeout_seconds wiring that was already correct. #516 done
    UW-L04 The readiness probe is unreachable from outside and returns a hardcoded 200, so it cannot report a database outage. Found 2026-08-04 while executing UW-F17. Every backend router except health declares its own /api/v1 prefix, and frontend/nginx.conf proxies only location /api/. api/health.py declares APIRouter(prefix="/health"), which is outside that prefix, so FastAPI's liveness and readiness handlers are not reachable through the ingress at all: /api/health/live returns a JSON 404 from FastAPI. Worse, the same nginx config defines location /health { return 200 'OK'; } for the frontend container's own probe, which shadows the path completely, so GET https://cyo.williamshome.family/health/ready answers with a two-byte text/plain OK from nginx (with a duplicated Content-Type header and server: nginx) while the readiness logic never executes. The consequence is not cosmetic: readiness is the check that proves database connectivity, so any external uptime monitor pointed at that URL reports healthy with the database completely down, and the R1 checklist recorded a "verified live 2026-07-07: both 200" pass on exactly that anti-oracle. /api/v1/me correctly returns 401 application/json, confirming the proxy itself is fine and the fault is the prefix mismatch plus the shadowing stub. Fixed 2026-08-04, not by the rename this row originally proposed. A rename alone would have caused an outage: the production backend healthcheck is defined out-of-repo in homelab-infra services/cyo-adventure/docker-compose.yml, probes /health/live, exits non-zero on any status >= 400, and is wired to depends_on: condition: service_healthy, so this repo deploying ahead of that one would have marked the container unhealthy rather than merely mis-pathed. Delivered additively instead: app.py mounts the health router twice, /api/v1/health/* as the canonical schema-visible form and /health/* as a schema-hidden alias, with the alias's retirement precondition recorded in a #CRITICAL marker at the mount site. middleware/security.py widened its HTTPS-redirect exemption to both prefixes, since a missed prefix there surfaces as a 307 that a probe reads as a failure, not as a 404. frontend/nginx.conf narrows its own stub to location = /nginx-health and, critically, returns an explicit 404 for /health rather than deleting the block: deleting it would let the path fall through to the SPA try_files fallback and answer 200 text/html, which is the same false pass wearing a different hat. Every probe in this repo (Dockerfile, docker-compose.yml, frontend/e2e-real/real-stack.ts, runbook section 3) now asserts content-type: application/json, so a static stub cannot forge a pass. #609 done
    UW-L05 Production holds a family row with no users and no contents. Found 2026-08-04 while executing UW-F17 section 6, which needed the real family inventory to tell a genuine isolation check from a vacuous one. Production has three family rows: 3a152319 (the real family, 2 profiles, 6 assignments, 1 story request, 5 device grants, 3 reading states), 84b96700 (the E2E test family, 1 profile, 5 assignments, 4 grants, 0 story requests, 0 reading states), and 0ca7a109, which has zero profiles, zero assignments, zero grants, zero requests, zero reading states, and zero user rows pointing at it. A family with no users is unreachable by design: every adult route resolves the caller's family through their user row, so nothing can read or write it and no console surface will ever list it. That is why it is recorded rather than urgent. It matters anyway for two reasons. First, guardian accounts are created by an empty POST /v1/onboarding (there is no signup form), so a family row with no user is the exact residue a partially-failed JIT provisioning would leave, and if that is the cause then the write is not atomic and a real guardian could hit it. Second, it inflates every family-count assertion, so a test that asserts "2 families" against production is already wrong and a test that asserts "3" encodes the anomaly as expected. To do: establish provenance before touching it, by checking whether created_at lines up with a known onboarding attempt or with an early manual seed, then either make the onboarding write atomic (transaction spanning family + user insert) or record it as a one-off seed artifact. Do not delete the row as the first action: while it exists it is the only evidence of how it came to exist. Re-checked 2026-08-08: a fourth family appeared, and it is the more informative of the two. adb9e257, created 2026-08-05 01:07 UTC, has zero profiles, assignments, grants, requests and reading states, but one guardian user row, unlike 0ca7a109's zero. That difference decides the provenance question this row was opened to answer: an empty family plus a user is what a completed empty onboarding leaves behind, whereas a family with no user is what a failed one leaves behind. So the two rows are probably not the same event, and the atomicity concern applies only to 0ca7a109. Check whether adb9e257's single user is a real person or a test sign-in before concluding either way; if it is real, the finding is a guardian who onboarded and never created a profile, which is a product observation rather than a defect. The real family also gained a third child profile in the same window, so both counts this register and the R1 checklist pin assertions to have now moved. Anything asserting a family count or a profile count against live production needs re-deriving; see the drift note in the checklist's Section 6. (no issue) unscheduled
    UW-L06 Retire the un-prefixed /health/* alias. app.py mounts the health router twice, /api/v1/health/* as the canonical schema-visible form and /health/* as a schema-hidden alias; the alias exists only so the out-of-repo production healthcheck keeps passing across the deploy. UW-L04 recorded the retirement precondition in a #CRITICAL marker at the mount site but scheduled no work to act on it, so a permanent second mount entered the codebase with a retirement plan nothing tracks. Raised in review of #609. Open prerequisite, and the reason this is blocked rather than unscheduled: homelab-infra services/cyo-adventure/docker-compose.yml probes /health/live, exits non-zero on any status >= 400, and is wired to depends_on: condition: service_healthy, so it must be repointed at /api/v1/health/live and redeployed before the alias is dropped. Dropping it first marks the container unhealthy rather than merely mis-pathed, which is the same outage the additive fix in UW-L04 was chosen to avoid. Retirement is then three deletions plus their tests: the second include_router call in app.py, the /health entry in _HEALTH_PATH_PREFIXES (middleware/security.py), and the location /health 404 block in frontend/nginx.conf, whose explicit 404 exists only to stop the SPA try_files fallback answering 200 text/html on the alias path. TestLegacyHealthPathAlias (tests/unit/test_app.py) and frontend/e2e-prod/health-probe.spec.ts both assert the alias's present behaviour, so they must be inverted rather than deleted; otherwise the retirement is assumed instead of proven. Also repoint the frontend container's out-of-repo external prober, which frontend/Dockerfile documents as curling bare /health and which this repo now answers with a 404 by design. #609 blocked
    UW-L07 Guardian profile resolution runs before the RLS context it depends on, so every guardian in production sees zero profiles. Found 2026-08-08 while executing UW-F17 against revision 631c0d8a (v0.68.0). api/deps.py::require_principal calls _resolve_profiles (line ~545) roughly 36 lines before apply_family_rls_context (line ~581). _resolve_profiles selects from child_profile, an ADR-022 Tier 1 family_scoped table whose policy is family_id::text = current_setting('app.family_id', true) OR current_setting('app.is_admin', true) = 'true'. With the GUC unset, current_setting(..., true) returns NULL, the predicate is unsatisfiable, and the read returns zero rows, so principal.profile_ids is empty for every guardian. GET /v1/profiles then short-circuits on it (api/profiles.py ~374) and the console renders "No profiles yet" over a family that has three children. This fails closed: it is an outage of the profiles surface, not a data leak, and the underlying rows are intact. Confirmed live: the 2026-08-07 e2e-prod run captured GET /api/v1/me returning "profile_ids":[] and GET /api/v1/profiles returning {"profiles":[]} while the database held the profiles. Why it stayed invisible until now: RLS never applies to a table's owner, so before the ADR-021 cutover the API connected as postgres and the policy was inert; cutting over to cyo_api (rolbypassrls=false) is what activated the ordering bug. Any bug of this shape is undetectable pre-cutover, which argues for testing as the non-owner role rather than for testing more. It is a recurrence, not a novel defect: _device_principal (defined at deps.py ~625) already applies the context before its Tier 1 reads and carries a #CRITICAL comment naming the identical staging outage of 2026-07-18 to 2026-08-02; that fix was applied to the device branch only. Sharper still, the file states the false invariant in its own words, one call site away from the bug. Two #CRITICAL blocks assert exactly what _resolve_profiles falsifies: the block above the apply_family_rls_context call (deps.py ~570) reasons that "two of the three branches resolve without needing the context this sets: guardian/admin read the user row just above under the Tier 2 blanket policy", and the block inside _device_principal (deps.py ~674) states that device_grant "is the ONLY pre-principal read of one". Both are false the moment _resolve_profiles reads child_profile at line ~545. The fix must correct these two comments as well, or the codebase keeps asserting a disproven invariant beside the corrected code, which is how the device-only half fix survived review the first time. Coverage gap that let it ship: tests/integration/test_rls_tier1_enforcement.py covers the device branch (test_device_principal_resolves_under_tier1_rls) with no guardian equivalent exercising the real require_principal path as cyo_api. Second-order finding, worth fixing with it: the cross-family isolation test (frontend/e2e-prod/guardian-books-and-isolation.spec.ts ~184) asserts the E2E family sees zero story requests; story_request is family_scoped too, so under this defect that assertion is satisfied by the bug, with its rendered-heading positive control fully intact. Only the profiles half, which expects a non-zero count, catches the failure. An emptiness assertion over a Tier 1 table needs a non-empty companion assertion under the same policy; a rendered-heading control proves the page loaded, never that scoping ran. Fix: apply the context before _resolve_profiles, keeping the existing later call, correct the two #CRITICAL comments above, and add the missing guardian regression test at the require_principal level rather than at the query level. Status caveat: PR #641 is open and implements exactly this fix over the same two files, so if it merges before this row does, the unscheduled status and the Fix paragraph are already historical; the deployed claims above stay accurate either way, since they are conditioned on production serving the fix rather than on the fix merging. #639 unscheduled
    UW-L08 Two published books, sk_vault_of_nine_iron (block/high, drowning) and sk_ninth_hand (flag/high, suicidal intent), hold severe-finding moderation reports and must be re-moderated once feat/moderation-review-hardening deploys. The 2026-08-25 review-process-hardening plan's D4 gate named both from its ground-truth section, which recorded sk_ninth_hand specifically as untracked anywhere; this row is now the tracking item for both books. The plan's Task 13 was to re-moderate both under the current pipeline after Tasks 1-10 deploy and put the fresh reports in front of the owner via the reviewer SOP's escalation section. Task 13 never ran in this execution: it depends on a deploy that has not happened, and no Task 13 brief was written (only Task 12's ops-sweep brief exists in .superpowers/sdd/). Owner to rule: the project owner, on unpublish-or-keep for each book once its fresh report is in hand (an owner-only content call per D4); both books stay published until then. Sequencing ratified 2026-08-25: no precautionary unpublish; after the branch deploys, re-moderate these two books first as targeted --book-id runs, review the fresh reports per the reviewer SOP's escalation section and make the D4 call per book, then run the full backfill sweep (runbook section 8). Scheduled-check posture ratified 2026-08-25: .github/workflows/moderation-report-health.yml's Monday cron stays running rather than being disabled or loosened; a red run every Monday until the 12 in-review books AL-624 records are re-moderated is an accepted, expected forcing function, not a defect in the check or its schedule. (This row's own two published books carry genuine block/high findings, not fail-safe artifacts, so moderation_report_unusable() does not flag them; they stay tracked here rather than by that workflow.) The workflow now carries the same cron-posture decision in a comment at its own file. (no issue) decision
    UW-L09 A provider response with empty content and finish_reason: 'stop' is classified transient and retried without bound. A validator drove the parsers live and showed that a content-block response yields dig_content=None with finish_reason='stop', which the retry policy reads as a transient failure of a request that can never succeed. UW-C235 (done) fixed the length arm for OpenRouter and UW-F44 carries the same shape for modal.py's token-budget exhaustion; neither covers the empty-content-with-stop arm, and UW-C309 covers only the content-filter half of the same symptom. Proposed, per W4 of the 2026-08-22 remediation plan: read finish_reason outside the adapter and map it once, with content_filter terminal under a distinct error, length a truncation error that triggers continuation rather than restart, and stop with empty content a hard error; surface a billed-but-empty counter; and add a parser test per provider for each finish_reason including this case, plus a test that a terminal classification does not retry. Expect a visible fall in apparent success rate when it ships, because requests that cannot succeed will fail once instead of retrying; announce it rather than let it read as a regression. (no issue) unscheduled

Fully re-diagnosed 2026-08-02; the original "rebuild the stale :staging image from b29aa6b" ask is refuted. That premise fails two ways: every migration since b29aa6b is purely additive (no DROP/RENAME COLUMN), so a forward-migrated database cannot break older ORM code, and trigger-image-build.yml has succeeded on every merge through 2026-08-02. The tier's redness was two independent faults. (1) Three deterministic 500s (/v1/notifications, /v1/review-queue, /v1/guardian/books, the only three routes issuing a full-ORM select(Storybook)/select(StorybookVersion)) caused by the 07-30 migration stall, i.e. the database being BEHIND a current backend, the exact inverse of the original diagnosis. Fixed by repair run 30754078625, confirmed in run 30757075720. (2) The device-grant 401: api/deps.py::_device_principal reads select(DeviceGrant).where(jti == ...) to learn the grant's family_id, but device_grant is an ADR-022 Tier 1 family_scoped table and apply_family_rls_context only runs AFTER the principal is built, so the lookup runs with app.family_id unset, matches zero rows fail-closed exactly as 20260724120000_scoped_rls_tier1_family_scoping.sql documents, and is reported as "device grant token failed verification". Not a secret, not a deployment fact: docker logs proves the SAME container minted (201) and rejected (401) in the same second. The comment at deps.py:571 claims there is no chicken-and-egg, which holds for guardian/admin (the user table has a Tier 2 blanket policy) and for child (no DB read at all) but was never extended to the device branch, the only pre-principal read of a Tier 1 table. Latent since the 2026-07-18 Tier 1 cutover and invisible to every other tier because local and e2e-real-* connect as the owner, which bypasses RLS. Code fix is in this repo on fix/device-grant-rls-lookup: _device_principal now applies the RLS context itself, from the token's already-verified family_id claim, before the lookup. A SECURITY DEFINER bypass function was drafted first and discarded: it would have made app code depend on a migration-only database object (absent from the ORM create_all test fixtures) and could not deploy until that migration landed, reintroducing the very migrate-before-deploy ordering hazard that caused fault (1) above. Setting context from the claim needs no migration, deploys as pure code, and additionally binds the grant row to the family the token claims, which the jti-only lookup never checked.     Cluster N: external scope-comparison findings¶

Full detail, citations, and field-level tables for each row live in external-scope-comparison-2026-08-05.md, since the source .docx files themselves live outside this repository and are not expected to persist. Each row below restates enough of that document to be actionable on its own, which means the same claim is written twice; the comparison document is authoritative on any conflict, and a correction to a row's reasoning belongs there first.

    ID Item Phase Status

    UW-N04 Widen the axe-core e2e a11y suite's tag scope from WCAG 2.1 AA (wcag2a/wcag2aa/wcag21a/wcag21aa) to WCAG 2.2 by adding both wcag22a and wcag22aa: axe's WCAG tags are additive per level, so wcag22aa alone covers only the 2.2 Level AA additions and would silently skip the Level A ones, consistent help (3.2.6) and redundant entry (3.3.7), which are the two criteria most relevant to consent and rights flows. In scope at AA: target size minimum (2.5.8), focus not obscured minimum (2.4.11), dragging movements (2.5.7), accessible authentication minimum (3.3.8).
