    UW-N05 Decide, before Track 2 phases 6-9 open intake to outside families, whether to keep ADR-005's admin-only-approves-every-story model or move to the external document's guardian-primary-approval-with-staff-escalation model. Admin-only review does not scale headcount-wise to a multi-family product; the alternative is only as safe as the moderation-calibration work already tracked under GS2/GS3. Owner to rule: the project owner, jointly with whoever holds the safety-operator role at the time. 7 decision
    UW-N06 Pin an ASVS level and a penetration-test surface list onto the ASVS 5.0.0 spine that docs/security/assurance-spine.md already names as the primary application-security framework, using the external document's NFR-SEC-01/NFR-SEC-16 target of Level 2 and its surface list (guardian/child/admin, API authorization, tenant isolation, payment/entitlement, export and deletion, AI-specific abuse) as the candidate scope statement for Phase 5's engagement (Cluster E). Do not adopt NFR-SEC-12's OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications as a control catalog: assurance-spine.md already classifies it, the API Top 10, and MITRE ATLAS as threat enumeration for corpus building, with the explicit note "Not control catalogs. Do not spine on them." Scoping refinement of an existing decision only; the actual gap closure is the existing Cluster E rows. 5 unscheduled
    UW-N07 Reconcile the external document's planning-default retention table against the already-resolved category-by-category schedule in docs/compliance/coppa-gdpr-remediation-plan.md ("Resolved 2026-07-20: accepted as drafted", kept as the actual policy and cited by ADR-018), rather than drafting from scratch: adopt the categories the resolved schedule omits (adult account/household, consent evidence, product analytics, application logs, financial records, backups), and settle the one row where the two disagree, reading progress, resolved as "life of the active profile plus 30-90 days after deactivation" against the external default of "target 12 months' inactivity." The resolved policy governs until deliberately changed. Owners to rule on the newly added categories: privacy counsel and finance. Feeds the same ADR-018 gate as UW-N02. Updated 2026-08-06: the reconciliation is done; the rulings are not. data-retention-policy.md now exists as the retention artifact ADR-018 D4 requires, drafted 2026-08-06 and still status: draft rather than published. It reproduces the 2026-07-20 resolved schedule verbatim rather than re-deriving it, settles the reading-progress row in favour of the resolved policy ("life of the active profile plus 30-90 days after deactivation" governs over the external 12-month-inactivity default), and adds all six omitted categories. Three of the added categories carry no window and are written as "Not yet set, owner ruling required" rather than being given an invented number: consent evidence, product analytics, and application logs. Financial records is marked not-applicable today (no payment data is collected) and needs a ruling before any payment feature ships. This row stays decision because those rulings are the remaining work. 7 decision
    UW-N08 ADR-027 decision 5's automated image-moderation classifier (the precondition for expanding per-node art past the seven-skeleton pilot) has no register home; its own text says only "tracked as follow-on work," which is the unscheduled-deferral pattern this register exists to close. Found while backfilling ADR-027's required Follow-on work section. Open prerequisite: the seven-skeleton pilot must run and its results must justify scaling before the classifier is worth building, which is why ADR-027's follow-on section calls this "blocked in effect on the pilot's own results per decision 5." 4b blocked
    UW-N09 Cite the Commercial and Revenue Viability Report's generated-image cost estimate (8-12 scenes ≈ $0.27-$2.40/story, public per-image rates) in ADR-027, whose own budget section priced storage and delivery only, never generation spend. Done: landed in ADR-027's new Follow-on work section in PR #620, the same change that added the comparison document. The figure is a per-story total derived from public per-image rates, not a per-image price, and it carries a scale caveat: 8-12 scenes is the external document's assumption, while ADR-027's own pilot scope is 10-45 node images per book, so it must be re-derived against 10-45 before informing any expand decision. 4b done
    UW-N10 Adopt the Commercial and Revenue Viability Report's "30-day Family Story Value Rate" north-star metric and its anti-gaming metric definitions (a completion requires a validated ending, not scrolling; a replay requires a deliberate alternate-path pick, not an accidental reload; unit cost includes support minutes) as this project's engagement/analytics measurement framework. Unblocked by commerce: no equivalent event-taxonomy or KPI doc exists in docs/planning/ today, and the framework is usable at the current family-tier, pre-monetization scale. 4c unscheduled
    UW-N11 Calibrate the LLM quality panel's per-criterion thresholds against the ten titles already sourced in docs/planning/data/craft-benchmark-corpus.yaml, rather than scaling the corpus first. W7 (cyo-measurement-workplan-2026-08-12.md) establishes that a criterion discriminates; nothing internal establishes what score is good, and external calibration is the only thing this corpus supplies that main's measurement machinery does not already do better. Two threshold-shaped findings are ready to use: character depth is weak or not_reported for 6 of 10 published titles and strong for none, so a weak character arc is a format norm rather than a verdict against generated stories; and replayability is strong for 7 of 10, so it is close to a format universal and a poor discriminator. Scaling toward about 30 titles is a dependent follow-on, not the headline: a 2026-08-30 re-verification pass found roughly a third of the accolade claims in the first batch did not survive a direct read of the cited page, so the sourcing method should not be run twenty more times before someone has established what the first ten are worth. 4c decision
    UW-N12 Rule on whether to pursue Tier A of the license-tiered IF corpus at all (real public-domain branching text as structural fixtures for validator/ and diversity/), and whether to contact the Lost Pig rights holder for written permission. docs/planning/if-corpus-license-tiers.md and docs/planning/data/if-corpus-licensing.yaml carry the verification work; both remaining questions are owner-gated and outward-facing. Note the 2026-08-30 re-verification pass downgraded two of five entries (The Interlopers adaptation to yellow, The Cavern of the Morlocks to reference_only), so the pool of candidate Tier A text is now one entry (Consider the Consequences!, public domain in the US by term expiry). Tier B (open children's-literature prose for reading-level calibration) has no title list and is out of scope. 5 decision
